## What's next — sequenced 2026-09-06, after `v0.119.1` shipped

**⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE SCANNING THE BACKLOG INDEX FOR CANDIDATES.** Seven plans were written 2026-09-05;
four have shipped (`v0.117.0`, `v0.118.0`, `v0.119.0`, `v0.119.1`). This section records what remains and
**in what order**, so a fresh session does not re-derive it from the plans themselves.

### 1. ✅ SHIPPED AS `v0.120.0` (Released 2026-09-06) — Canonical note title integrity (`docs/claude-plans/canonical-note-title-generation-integrity-stage1.md`)

**⚠️ NO LONGER A CANDIDATE — IT SHIPPED.** With it done, **item 2 below is now the head of the queue** (and is blocked on an owner conversation, not on engineering). **⚠️ THE ALE REVIEW SET CURATOR PASS IS NOW FULLY UNBLOCKED:** its ordering constraint was this fix, so new ALE notes can be authored through Bulk Generate without acquiring contaminated titles. The three owner decisions below were taken at
kickoff on 2026-09-06; **(a) added an opt-in suggestion AGAINST the recommendation, and (c) was first
taken in and then DEFERRED on a finding** (there is no title-producing call to narrow — see the Current
Release Baseline and (c)'s own Backlog row). **Read the baseline, not this section, for what shipped.**
What follows is the candidate framing as written on 2026-09-06, kept because its ordering argument and
anti-drift still bind.

**The only remaining item that is a LIVE, REPRODUCED DEFECT ON CANONICAL CONTENT rather than a plan for
a capability.** A curator entered `Site Grading Principles` in Bulk Generate and the persisted title
became `Site Grading Principles in Civil Engineering` — with Civil Engineering **not** among the four
selected Applicable Programs.

**⚠️ THE CAUSE IS NEITHER OF THE TWO SUSPECTED ONES, AND RE-DERIVING THEM IS WASTED WORK.** It is not
context contamination and not a missing prompt rule; the audit closes both (§3, §4). It is one
unconditional mutation: **Bulk Generate creates the Note with the curator's topic as its title, then
overwrites it with the STUDY PACK's generated title** — `applyBulkGeneratedMetadataToNote` does
`note.setTitle(normalizeEditableTitle(generated.title()))`. Single-note generation never does this.
**The fix deletes a later mutation rather than adding logic.**

**⚠️ WHY IT IS ORDERED FIRST, AND IT IS AN ORDERING ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A SEVERITY ONE.** `v0.118.0`'s
locked contract is *"the Note's current title is the topic"*, so a contaminated title becomes the **seed
for regenerated content** — `…in Civil Engineering` would shape the body of every future regeneration of
that note. **Fix titles before regenerating the canonical library**, or the ALE pass has to be redone.

**⚠️ EXISTING NOTES ARE SAFE TODAY — verified in code during `v0.119.0`, not assumed.**
`applyBulkGeneratedMetadataToNote` is **unreachable from regeneration**: the bulk driver passes
`preservedSubject = null` deliberately, and single-note regeneration passes
`autoApplyGeneratedMetadata = false`. The exposure is **authoring NEW notes via Bulk Generate**.

**Three genuine owner decisions, each with the audit's recommendation** — take them at kickoff rather
than mid-implementation: (a) should the curator topic always win, or should an explicit *"suggest a
better title"* action exist? *(recommend: topic wins; no suggestion action — that is a feature, not part
of stopping the regression)*; (b) what happens to LLM tags once the title is not applied? *(recommend:
keep them, derived from the curator's topic rather than the discarded generated title)*; (c) is the
narrowed title-input contract wanted? *(recommend: defer — the context was already clean, so it would
not have prevented this)*.

**⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT FROM THE AUDIT: do NOT add Course/Program suffixes by default, and do NOT infer a title
from Applicable Programs, Review Set, Subject Plan or Domain Context.** No migration, no renames of
existing notes, no regeneration pass — the audit is explicit that Stage 1 covers none of those.

### 2. ✅ SHIPPED AS `v0.121.0` (Released 2026-09-06) — shared quiz recipient experience

**⚠️ NO LONGER A BLOCKED CANDIDATE — THE OWNER CONVERSATION HAPPENED AND THE RELEASE IS OPEN.** §1's three contradictions were read and resolved at kickoff, and the slice-4 provenance rule was owner-ruled the same day; **read the Current Release Baseline above, not this section, for what is being built.** `v0.121.0` takes **slices 1-4**; **slice 5 (Learning Connection integration) remains a separate follow-up release** and the supporter-progress audit travels with it. What follows is the candidate framing as written 2026-09-06, kept because its pairing rule and its "read §1 first" warning still bind.

**⚠️ SLICE 5 (`Learning Connection integration`) IS DEFERRED, NOT CANCELLED, AND IT IS GATED ON A DATE (2026-09-06).** It was the natural next release and was declined on evidence: a read-only production query returned **1 `linked_learner_relationships` row, 1 `ACCEPTED`, 2 invitations, 3 live grants**, so slice 5 would enrich a **supporter** surface for a population of one. **⚠️ TWO SEPARATE THINGS GATE IT AND THEY MUST NOT BE COLLAPSED. (a) EVIDENCE:** `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]`'s own instruction on a non-zero `ACCEPTED` count is ***"re-read at the next release rather than expand"*** — so slice 5 waits on that re-read. **(b) CONTAMINATION:** that same read is **13 days out** and its kill criterion keys on `ACCEPTED`, so **`v0.122.0` must not promote Learning Connections at all** — not the nav position, not a guidance tip. **That half unblocks after `2026-09-19` regardless of what the re-read concludes.** **⚠️ ONE PIECE OF SLICE 5 IS SHARPER THAN THE REST AND MUST NOT DRIFT INTO A "SMALL COPY RELEASE" LATER: §7's SENDER CONTEXT LOOKS LIKE COPY AND IS NOT.** It resolves a quiz owner's display name onto an **anonymous `permitAll` payload**, gated on a relationship predicate — an identity disclosure whose correctness IS a visibility predicate. **Whenever it is built it carries its own verification tier (ONE SCOPED COLD AGENT framed as falsification), and it is explicitly OUT of `v0.122.0`.**

#### Original candidate framing — shared quiz recipient experience + supporter progress

`docs/claude-plans/shared-quiz-recipient-experience-plan.md` and
`docs/claude-plans/supporter-progress-visibility-audit.md` are **companions and must be read together**;
the recipient plan's §8 supersedes the supporter audit where they differ.

**⚠️ DO NOT KICK EITHER OFF AS-IS.** The recipient plan opens by stating that **three owner decisions are
contradicted by the repo** — not disagreements with the direction, but *missing substrate the direction
assumes exists*. **Read its §1 before anything else.** That is an owner conversation first and a release
second.

### 3. EXPLICITLY NOT SCHEDULED — quiz assignments

`docs/claude-plans/quiz-assignments-learning-connections-stage1.md` is marked **NOT IMPLEMENTED, NOT

## Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame (added 2026-07-28 — read before scoping or reprioritizing anything below)

> ### Ratified product vision — 2026-08-05. Read this first; it governs everything below.
>
> **NoteLib is a learning system built on top of a knowledge library.** The library is the foundation; **the learning journey is the product.**
>
> Three layers, earned strictly in order:
>
> 1. **Trust** — comprehensive Official Review Sets, so a learner can say *"I can trust NoteLib to cover my review."*
> 2. **Habit** — Study Packs, Companion, Progress, and Review Sets forming the system they return to.
> 3. **Community** — high-quality user-created and shared knowledge, once the first two are earned.
>
> **This confirms and sharpens the "journey, not the note" frame below rather than replacing it.** What it adds is the *ordering*, and one prioritization consequence: **community content is no longer the primary acquisition strategy.** Long-term, users creating and sharing their own notes is still absolutely the goal — the vision is not abandoned, it is being earned in the correct order. Only after a learner trusts NoteLib's coverage does the product earn the right to become their personal knowledge library.
>
> **What this means when scoping:** work that deepens Official Review Set comprehensiveness or the learning system outranks work that grows user-generated content as a funnel. Do not propose UGC as top-of-funnel acquisition; do not read the de-emphasis as the community feature being cut. Full statement in `docs/product/SPEC.md` → Product Philosophy.

This section exists so a fresh session doesn't have to re-derive from scratch what "NoteLib's center of gravity has shifted toward the learner's journey, not the note" actually means for prioritization. It was added after a strategic reprioritization discussion prompted by noticing that recent releases (Dashboard, Progress, Companion, Coach, Official Review Sets, Reusable Practice Assets) skew journey-centric rather than notes-first.

**This is not a new pivot — it's a long-held bet becoming visible, and internal strategy is already ahead of it.** "A Note Collection is a trackable readiness journey, not a folder" has been the stated direction since v0.33.0; "NoteLib as a personal learning OS" was ratified internally with GPT on 2026-07-23 (5 days before this note), as part of the Company Redefinition effort (see that section above). What changed isn't the direction — it's that enough journey-layer surface area now exists to see the shape in aggregate. Treat this as confirmation the existing direction is compounding, not a trigger to re-litigate it.

**The one real, un-actioned gap this surfaced: external marketing copy hasn't caught up to internal strategy.** The landing page still says "your notes become your study system" (notes-first); internal strategy has said learning-OS-centric for 5 days. See the Backlog Index row below — since superseded by the **Messaging Architecture** row. **Two parts of this note are now out of date:** the conversion-test condition was explicitly lifted for positioning copy by owner ratification 2026-08-01 (narrative consistency with the product vision is a design decision, not an optimization experiment; conversion is measured post-launch instead of gating rollout — pricing/checkout *mechanics* changes still need evidence), and "small, not urgent" undersold it — the ratified initiative is product-wide. The landing page itself is still un-actioned and is now tracked in that row's remaining-surfaces list.

**Prioritization filter — use to triage new candidates, weighted, not flat.** Does a candidate move: (1) acquisition, (2) return rate, (3) study effectiveness, (4) reusable practice assets, (5) the creator ecosystem, or (6) does it fix something broken / protect trust (bug fixes, data integrity, paywall correctness — always in scope regardless of the other five)? While retention (2.4% W1→W2, unmoved across three prior attempts) remains the binding constraint, (2) should dominate; (1) and (3)-(5) are tie-breakers, not co-equal boxes to check. Don't let "this touches box N a little" alone justify a build — that's how feature creep re-enters.

**Guardrail: don't let Challenge Quiz's recent momentum keep pulling the next discretionary pick.** The heavy Challenge Quiz investment this cycle (v0.60.1/v0.60.2/v0.60.3/v0.61.0) was mostly bug-driven, not strategy-driven — v0.60.0's template sharing surfaced a real production bug cluster that had to get fixed, and familiarity kept pulling more work into that same corner afterward. That work was necessary and is now done; Challenge Quiz is stable. The next discretionary pick after v0.61.0 should default toward between-session/Coach/Progress work (the actual proven bottleneck — see the "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc) unless a new production bug forces otherwise.

**Loops, not just features — see the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc**, which now names three loops (Acquisition, Learning, Creator) and is honest about which ones are actually running today versus asserted or unproven. Use it as a diagnostic — naming something a loop doesn't make it spin.

## Backlog Index (read this before deciding what's next — do not scan prose to find candidates)

**Invariant: no PLANNING OR CURATION ARTIFACT may exist anywhere in `docs/` without a row here** — `docs/claude-prompt/*-out/` directories, `docs/claude-plans/` files, `docs/claude-findings/` reports, and `docs/curriculum/` workbooks and queries alike. **⚠️ THE RULE IS STATED BY PROPERTY NOW, BECAUSE NAMING DIRECTORIES HAS BEEN OUTGROWN TWICE.** It first named only `*-out/`, and the `v0.71.1` kickoff found five unindexed `docs/claude-plans/` files; it was widened to name that directory too, and the `v0.97.0` signoff then found six unindexed files in a **new** `docs/curriculum/`, which the rule did not mention. A list of directories is always one new directory behind. **The test is what the file IS — does anything remain to be done with it — not where it lives.** A Fable session that writes planning output adds its row in the same commit. This rule exists because Smart Review Planning (7 fully-architected documents in `docs/claude-prompt/fable-out/`) sat undiscovered for ~5 release cycles, found only by chance when the user asked about it directly — a prose mention buried in a released-version paragraph is not an index entry.

**One narrow exemption, stated 2026-08-10 at the `v0.71.1` kickoff rather than left to be re-derived: a *release artifact* is covered by its version's own row and does not need one of its own.** A release artifact is a file written to serve one release's decision and then finished — a consultation prompt sent to GPT, a sizing query run once against production, an audit result. It is not planning output waiting to be scheduled, which is what this invariant exists to catch. The distinction is *does anything remain to be done with it*, not which directory it lives in. Currently exempt, each traceable through its release's section above: `b2-migration-artifact-sizing.sql` and `c1-multi-program-null-domain-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.0`), **⚠️ plus two that were exempt and are now DELETED in the `v0.97.0` docs cleanup, 2026-08-29** — `b2-learner-owned-applicable-programs-consultation-prompt.md` and `onboarding-intent-router-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md`, both `v0.71.0`, both answered and shipped (B2 became `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*; the Intent Router became `v0.71.0` slice 5, whose live row cites its **plan** file and audit SQL, not the prompt). **⚠️ THIS SENTENCE IS A LIVE REGISTRY, NOT A DATED LOG, which is why it is edited rather than left standing** — the historical-log-entry ruling (leave dated scan notes intact) does not extend to a list that asserts what is exempt *now*; leaving it would have claimed exemption for files that no longer exist. Also `item1-admin-authored-applicable-programs-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.1`, run 2026-08-11, results recorded in `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*), plus `v0.67.0-explore-owns-discovery-ia.md` (`v0.67.0`, shipped in full). **Added 2026-09-05 at the `v0.119.0` kickoff scan, after a naive basename grep flagged all four:** `authoring-and-quiz-legibility-gpt-tightening-prompt.md` (`v0.117.0`, a GPT tightening prompt, answered and shipped) and `v0.115.0-stage1-adoption-update-audit.md`, `v0.115.0-stage1-domain-context-audit.md`, `v0.115.0-stage1-note-copy-audit.md` (`v0.115.0`, audit results for a Released version whose live row cites `v0.115.0-stage1-master-architecture-reconciliation.md`). **If a `.sql` or consultation prompt ever carries an unrun query or an unanswered question past its release's signoff, it stops being an artifact and needs a row.**

**Cite rows by TITLE, never by line number.** Added 2026-08-14 after the `v0.75.0` pressure test found three citations pointing at the wrong rows: `RELEASES.md` and this file cited "row 185" and "row 187", both correct when written, both wrong once the kickoff commit inserted two lines above the index. **This recurs by construction** — every kickoff edits text above this table — and one of the broken pointers was the stated evidence for a design decision, so the reader would have landed on an unrelated row and found no support for it.

**⚠️ COUNTING THIS TABLE MECHANICALLY IS UNRELIABLE, AND THE FAILURE IS REPEATABLE — added 2026-09-03 after deriving THREE different counts of the same September cluster in one session.** Two traps, both hit: **(a) ROWS ARE NOT A FIXED WIDTH.** Most have 5 content cells, but several carry `|` inside their prose — the Retention H1+H5 row has **7** — so `awk '{print $(NF-1)}'` and `cells[-2]` read **Last reviewed**, not **Gate**. That mis-read produced a confident claim that `2026-09-10` *"is not a gate date at all"*, which is **FALSE**: it is `v0.72.0`'s live proximal retention checkpoint. **A correction based on it was written and discarded before it reached the record only because the row was opened and read.** **(b) A MENTION IS NOT AN OBLIGATION.** Rows cross-reference each other's checkpoints — *"onboarding stays frozen until `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`"* is a constraint citing a date, not a read owed by that row — so grepping dates over-counts, while grepping only titles under-counts the ones declared in the Gate cell. **⚠️ THE RELIABLE METHOD IS TO OPEN THE ROW.** Establish per row whether it OWNS a dated read or merely cites one; do not report a count derived by pattern alone, and do not "correct" a previous count without opening the rows behind both. **A wrong correction is worse than the wrong number, because it arrives with the authority of a fix.**

**Review ritual:** every `/kickoff`, scan this table — bump `Last reviewed`, check whether any `Gate` condition became true, **apply the consolidation rule below to any checkpoint written since the last kickoff** (a new dated read joins an existing batch date unless it earns its own by the stated criteria), verify every `docs/claude-prompt/` planning directory, **every `docs/claude-plans/` file AND every `docs/claude-findings/` file** still has a row or falls under the artifact exemption above (see kickoff checklist step 8 in `CLAUDE.md`), and scan for `CHECKPOINT` rows past their due date (see kickoff checklist step 9).

**⚠️ THE CLUSTER FIGURE CARRIED BY THE LAST FOUR SCAN NOTES WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION.** Every scan from `v0.103.0` through `v0.106.0` recorded *"27 rows on `2026-09-11`"*. **That is a MENTION count, not a row count** — a `grep -c` over a file whose Backlog Index intro is itself thick with prose cross-references to those dates, so the scan notes were counting their own predecessors. Re-derived here by parsing the table body row by row (159 table lines; **142 live, 9 struck**) and reading each row's own title and `Gate` cell: **32 LIVE rows touch `2026-09-10`–`2026-09-17`**, and the useful split is not the total but the KIND — **~12 are CHECKPOINT READS** (production SQL with kill criteria already written: `v0.72.0` retention H1+H5 on `2026-09-10`; `v0.74.0` ×2 on `2026-09-12`; `v0.79.0`, `v0.78.0`, `v0.80.0` on `2026-09-14`; `v0.81.0` on `2026-09-15`; `v0.82.0` ×2, `v0.83.0`, `v0.84.0` on `2026-09-16`; `v0.85.0` on `2026-09-17`) and **~20 are PARKED WORK ITEMS** the window unblocks (the whole onboarding set, Target Audience Phases 2–4, Discovery System, the LaTeX rendering gap). **⚠️ THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT: the cluster is a READING workload, not a building one**, which is what made it safe to scope a feature release across it rather than reserving a slot. **Recorded at length because a wrong number was carried forward four times unchallenged, and it was the stated input to a sequencing decision each time** — the exact status-staleness failure this intro keeps warning about, appearing inside step 9's own bookkeeping for the second time.

**⚠️ ONE LOAD-BEARING FINDING, UN-ACTIONED SINCE IT WAS WRITTEN, AND IT IS FOLDED INTO THIS RELEASE.** The ratified *Adaptive Practice becomes the recommendation engine* row records that **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]`'s kill criterion instructs a reader to "restore a demoted entry point"** if Adaptive Practice adoption fell — which **directly contradicts the direction that row ratifies**, and which `v0.107.0` item 2 implements. The row's own words: it *"must be re-specified now, or it will fire in September and someone will correctly follow an instruction that undoes a ratified direction."* **Nobody re-specified it, and it fires in 9 days, inside this release's window.** It is folded in as item 3 — **docs-only, and as a DEPENDENCY of item 2 rather than a third feature.** **⚠️ Note what this means about the gate: step 9 scans for OVERDUE rows, so a checkpoint that is merely WRONG scans clean until the day it fires.** It was found by reading the direction row against its checkpoint, not by the date scan.

The row is amended to carry that.

**Gate types, added 2026-07-28 (pressure-tested via advisor + an independent Opus review — see the "Prioritization Lens" section above for why this was needed).** Every `Gate` cell below should read as one of four types, tagged inline. The problem this fixes: an EVIDENCE gate and a DECISION gate both render as "blocked," but only one is actually waiting on data — a DECISION gate is unblockable by more querying, it just needs the owner to choose. Conflating them makes the whole roadmap feel uniformly data-blocked when a real chunk of it is one decision away.

- **`[EVIDENCE]`** — genuinely needs production data or a real-world signal before building. The strict-gating discipline applies in full.
- **`[DECISION]`** — waiting on an explicit owner call, not on more data. Actionable today by having the conversation, not by running a query.
- **`[EFFORT]`** — needs a scoping pass, curator capacity, or non-engineering execution (outreach, a design pass). Not data-blocked, resource- or attention-blocked.
- **`[CHECKPOINT — due YYYY-MM-DD]`** — an item that shipped ahead of evidence (an `EVIDENCE` gate was invalid — see the bootstrap test below) and carries a dated, falsifiable post-build obligation instead of a pre-build gate. Must have all five: a date, a named kill criterion, live in this column (not buried in Status prose), scanned by the review ritual above, and its measurement instrumentation shipped in the same release — a checkpoint without a working metric is decorative (see `08-diagnostic-read-methodology.md`'s v0.48.0 digest-lift postmortem for what an unmeasurable checkpoint looks like in practice).

**The bootstrap test — when an `EVIDENCE` gate is actually invalid and the item should ship as `DECISION` + `CHECKPOINT` instead.** All three must hold, not just one:
1. The feature itself is the mechanism that would produce the gate's own metric (not merely correlated with it) — e.g. Knowledge Impact is the only lever that rewards publishing, so waiting for publish-rate evidence before building the thing that would move publish rate is circular. (Contrast: Bulk Quiz's teacher-count gate is *not* invalid this way — teachers sign up for reasons unrelated to Bulk Quiz, so "we can't know until it exists" doesn't apply.)
2. It fits one release slot and is reversible — no schema/pricing/nav commitment that needs a migration to withdraw. This clause is what stops the bootstrap argument from justifying unlimited spend.
3. The checkpoint metric will actually be operable at current scale in the stated window — not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives. This is the clause that would have caught Knowledge Impact's original re-check design (re-running the same 3-creator-count query) before it shipped; the fix is a conditional-rate event (did a creator who viewed their impact dashboard publish again within N days), not a raw count.

**⚠️ CHECKPOINT CONSOLIDATION — 2026-08-16. Read this before acting on any dated row below.** The table had accumulated **~24 dated obligations across 18 rows, spread over nine separate September dates.** That is not a discipline, it is overhead: nine reading sessions against one analytics table, in one window, at a scale where most of the rates involved have denominators in the tens. **This applies clause 3 of the bootstrap test retroactively** — it was always the rule (*"operable at current scale … not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives"*), it was simply never enforced against checkpoints already written.

**Nine dates collapse to two. The per-row metrics, kill criteria and caveats below all still stand — only the date changes.**

- **`2026-09-11` — the onboarding funnel read, alone.** It keeps its own date because it has a real denominator (375 signups against a 62.4% completion baseline), it gates concrete work (the onboarding catalog-first follow-up), and reading it early is worthless. Rows: onboarding redesign, catalog-first-in-onboarding.
- **`2026-09-16` — everything else, as ONE batch.** Queries are already written in `docs/claude-plans/september-2026-checkpoint-reads.sql`. **Query 0 (delivery health) is read first and conditions all the rest**, which is itself an argument for batching: run separately, eight of these reads are interpreted before the number that tells you whether their events even arrived. Rows: `v0.78.0`, `v0.79.0` (proximal), `v0.80.0`, `v0.81.0`, `v0.82.0`, `v0.74.0` ×3, post-mastery next step, Adaptive Practice entry attribution.
- **Unchanged:** distal reads (`2026-10-15`, `2026-11-09`), far-future architectural dates (`2027-02-01`), and the `2026-09-30` Challenge Quiz adoption reads — different windows, not the same batch.

**What earns its own date from here on.** Being wrong is expensive and hard to reverse (a migration that writes data, anything that destroys evidence — `v0.82.0`'s is the model), **or** the denominator is genuinely large (funnel rates over all signups, total event volume). **What does not:** a conversion rate on a surface only a handful of learners reach. Write those down, batch them, and accept that *"too small to read"* is the likely finding — it is still a finding, it just does not deserve a calendar entry of its own.

**⚠️ Two gates were converted to recorded confounds rather than kept, 2026-08-16 (owner call).** The Target Audience retirement and the academic-levels program cleanup were both gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]` because they change Public Library discovery. **They now ship without waiting**, with the confound written down instead — the same call `v0.78.0` made and recorded. The reasoning: since `v0.79.0` the course/program chips are the primary filter vocabulary and audience is secondary, so removing it is a marginal confound rather than a fatal one. **This does NOT extend to dropping `notes.target_profile_type`**, which stays until `2026-09-16` reports — it is the input `V117` derived depth from, and dropping it would make that checkpoint's own kill criterion unexecutable.

The arithmetic itself is not in doubt.** **⚠️ AND THE AUDIT UNDER-COUNTED ITS OWN LEVER 1: it named the detail page's `listNotes()` but `v0.123.0` fixed only the BUILDER, while `RELEASES.md` claimed both — corrected in place at the `v0.124.0` signoff. `v0.124.0` closed the detail-page half.** **⚠️ **⚠️ CLOSED BY `v0.125.0` — THE WHOLE AUDIT IS NOW SPENT.** Sequencing 3's bound was gated on server-side picker search, which `v0.125.0` built (`GET /notes` gained an additive `search` parameter over title, subject, course program and tags), so the picker is now bounded at 50 **with** search beside it — never a bound alone, which is the shape `v0.123.0` refused. | **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-07]` — has the shared-quiz capability been PROMOTED, so that any recipient metric has a denominator at all?** | `v0.121.0` signoff | **⚠️ THIS IS THE PROXIMAL TIER, AND IT DELIBERATELY MEASURES A PRODUCT ACTION RATHER THAN A RATE — because at signoff there was no denominator to read.** A read-only production query on 2026-09-06 returned **8 `generated_quizzes` from ONE distinct generator, 1 share link, 0 combined quizzes**. `v0.121.0` is therefore **fix-before-promote**: it repaired a recipient path (recipients were scored on matching questions they could not answer, and could not correct a mistake) that nobody had reached yet. **⚠️ Reading an opened→completed rate now would reproduce the `v0.109.0` error of measuring OUR OWN SILENCE and calling it demand.** Metric: has the capability been surfaced anywhere a learner or supporter would find it — and does `quiz_share_links` show **≥20 links from ≥5 distinct generators**, the floor below which the distal tier cannot be read? | **KILL CRITERION, stated before the read: if the capability has NOT been promoted by this date, the distal checkpoint is RE-DATED, never answered — a zero completion rate on an unpromoted capability is not evidence about the recipient experience.** If promotion has happened and the floor is still unmet, the question becomes whether shared quizzes are wanted at all, which is a product decision and not this row's to take. **⚠️ UPDATED AT `v0.122.0` SIGNOFF (2026-09-06) — THE PROMOTION HALF OF THE METRIC IS NOW SATISFIED, so this read no longer has to establish it: a one-time note-detail tip (`note-detail-quiz-for-someone`, firing on a quiz-ready note with no generated quiz) plus a line on `app/page.tsx` and `app/how-it-works/page.tsx`. **Only the `≥20 links from ≥5 distinct generators` floor remains to be read.** **⚠️ AND THE 'NOT WANTED' INFERENCE NOW HAS ITS DENOMINATOR, WHICH IT DID NOT AT FIRST DELIVERY: `v0.122.0` shipped the tip with `trackAnalytics` OFF, and the signoff checkpoint gate caught it — the outcome metric alone cannot separate *nobody was told* from *nobody wanted it*, the same confound that made `v0.109.0` LIFT two dated reads. `GUIDANCE_TIP_SHOWN` now fires with `tipId=note-detail-quiz-for-someone` (verified emitting, mutation-verified both directions), so an unmet floor can be read against how many learners actually SAW the announcement. ⚠️ Do NOT conclude *not wanted* from an unmet floor without checking that impression count first.** | 2026-09-06 |
| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-12-04]` — do recipients who OPEN a shared quiz now FINISH it?** | `v0.121.0` signoff | **THE DISTAL TIER, AND THE ONE THE RELEASE'S OWN THESIS RESTS ON.** `v0.121.0` argues the recipient experience was the thing suppressing use: matching questions were unanswerable yet scored, answers were append-only so a mistake could not be corrected, the CTA overflowed on mobile, and signed-in recipients got the app shell mid-assessment. Metric: **`QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_OPENED` → `QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_COMPLETED` rate.** **⚠️ THE DENOMINATOR MUST BE FILTERED OR THE RATE READS LOW, AND THIS IS PRE-EXISTING MIS-INSTRUMENTATION RATHER THAN A `v0.121.0` regression — found by the slice 1-3 falsification pass, recorded BEFORE the read:** `QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_OPENED` fires twice from the recipient page — once on load with `metadata = {token}`, and again from the results-screen signup CTA with `metadata.source = "shared_quiz_results_signup_cta"`. The second fires **only after a recipient has already completed**, so counting both inflates the denominator with a subset of completers. **Count only events where `metadata.source` IS NULL.** The CTA's event type was deliberately NOT changed — this release's anti-drift forbids moving `QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_OPENED`'s firing conditions. **⚠️ INSTRUMENTATION SHIPPED IN THIS RELEASE AND IS VERIFIED EMITTING** — `QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_COMPLETED` fires at `frontend/app/quiz/[token]/page.tsx:176`, **only after grading succeeds**, and two tests pin both that it fires on success and that it does NOT fire when grading fails. `QUIZ_SHARE_LINK_OPENED` already fired from the same page. | **KILL CRITERION: if the opened→completed rate is materially below ~60% once the denominator floor is met, the remaining drop-off is NOT the four defects this release fixed, and the next step is to instrument WHERE recipients abandon (question index at exit) rather than to keep polishing the same screen.** **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE: gated on the proximal row's ≥20-links/≥5-generators floor. Below it, *not yet measurable* is a RE-DATE, not a verdict** — this is the `v0.72.0` underpowered-read shape, where a cohort of 31 gave P(zero \| no change) = 54.3%. | 2026-09-06 |

(b) Owner decision 1 admitted **TEACHER** curators against the plan's ADMIN-only recommendation, which is why this release fired the full three-agent tier — **a single action can spend up to 50 note-generation + 50 Study Pack units on a PAID account, and nobody has measured whether a TEACHER's monthly allowance survives one real batch.** Metric: `BULK_REGENERATION_STARTED`, counted overall and split on its `metered` field, with `requestedCount` for batch size. **⚠️ INSTRUMENTATION ADDED AT SIGNOFF BECAUSE THE GATE CAUGHT ITS ABSENCE — there was NO durable signal at all: `note_bulk_regeneration_item` is a receipt with a 24 h TTL, and nothing else distinguishes a bulk-regenerated note from a single-note one, since both mutate `notes.updated_at` in place. Verified EMITTING, not merely present in the enum, by `queueingABatchEmitsTheEventTheCheckpointsRead`, which asserts the `metered` field rather than just the event name.** | **KILL CRITERION, stated before the read: if `metered=true` batches are routinely BLOCKED on quota — i.e. TEACHER curators cannot finish a batch they started — then admitting TEACHERs without an allowance change was the wrong call, and the decision is RE-OPENED rather than patched by widening the ADMIN bypass, which anti-drift forbids.** **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE: the curator population is tiny and bulk is one action per Review Set rebuild, so a low count is EXPECTED and is a RE-DATE, not a verdict. Metric: `BULK_REGENERATION_STARTED` timestamps against whether the owner reported losing a receipt they wanted. | **KILL CRITERION: if a curator reports a receipt gone before they finished acting on it, EXTEND the TTL rather than adding permanent audit history — §H rules that out explicitly. ⚠️ Do NOT extend it pre-emptively on this row's existence alone; that is the drift this checkpoint exists to prevent.** | 2026-09-06 |

| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-05]` — do learners APPLY offered updates, and does the apply path put the connection pool back under load?** | `v0.116.0` signoff | **⚠️ TWO QUESTIONS ON ONE DATE, AND THE SECOND IS THE SHARPER ONE.** **(a) UPTAKE — shipped on a DEMAND INFERENCE, not a measurement:** 10 no-op re-adopt attempts by 4 distinct users were read as learners asking for updates. Suggestive, not uptake — those presses may equally have been attempts to RESET a plan, or mis-clicks on a button that appeared to do nothing. **(b) LOAD — an apply holds ONE pooled connection for the entire request** (`DELAYED_ACQUISITION_AND_HOLD`, open-in-view ON, read from `ConnectionLifetimeStartupLogger` rather than framework defaults), up to **1,204 `copyNote` calls** if every learner applies, against `maximum-pool-size: 20` and a 5 s acquisition timeout — **and `inspectSourceUpdate` is N+1 by plan and runs on EVERY page load of all 523 adopted collections.** This is a new long-holding request path added **one release after the 2026-09-04 pool-exhaustion outage.** Query written at signoff: `docs/claude-plans/v0.116.0-additive-updates-checkpoint-read.sql` (Q1 uptake, **Q2 the offer side that makes Q1 interpretable**, Q3 the restructure population, Q4 item-4 reachability). | **KILL CRITERIA, STATED BEFORE THE READ. (a) If learners are OFFERED updates and essentially never apply them, the additive mechanism is not what they wanted — do NOT build Slices 4-5 on top of it; re-open what the re-adopt presses actually meant first. (b) If 5xx or acquisition-timeout rates rise materially, the apply path must move off a single request-scoped connection BEFORE further work lands on it** — that is the outage mechanism, not a slow page. **⚠️ Q2 IS WHAT MAKES Q1 INTERPRETABLE and must not be skipped:** a low apply rate against a low offer rate means nothing was ever offered, which is NOT learner disinterest. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE: 92 learners hold adoptions and only 28 have pending additions. "Too few to read" is a RE-DATE for (a) — and itself a finding, since it would mean the stranded population is smaller than 364 stranded placements implied. It is NEVER a re-date for (b): a load problem does not need a large denominator to be real.** **⚠️ Q3 IS A REGRESSION TRIPWIRE, NOT A METRIC:** an adoption holding direct notes whose source has children must report MOVED and apply nothing — if any gained children, the fix regressed and that is a P0. | 2026-09-05 |

| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-05]` — did clearing TRANSFER classification authority, or just remove representation?** | `v0.115.0` signoff | **SHIPPED ON A BOOTSTRAP ARGUMENT FROM MECHANISM, AND THAT IS WHY IT OWES A READ.** The release's central claim is that clearing the copied rows **un-shadows** the learner's own Course / Program field, which the editor then renders with a required marker (`note-editor-form.tsx:427-433`) — so authority is **transferred**, not merely revoked. **That the learner then actually classifies is INFERRED FROM THE MECHANISM, never measured.** If they publish and never fill it in, the release traded *shelved under the curator's programs* for **not shelved at all** — a discovery regression wearing the shape of a correctness fix. **⚠️ SHARES ITS DATE with the row above by the consolidation rule; the subjects are unrelated and the reads run in one sitting.** **Instrumentation needs nothing new and was VERIFIED FIRING at signoff:** `PUBLIC_NOTE_PUBLISHED` (`NoteService:505`) fires on the **identical** `becomingPublic` predicate as the clearing, so the post-deploy cohort is exactly identifiable, and the outcome reads straight off `notes` + `note_course_program`. Query written at signoff: `docs/claude-plans/v0.115.0-publication-authority-checkpoint-read.sql` (Q1 headline, Q2 time-to-classify, **Q3 the control**, Q4 catalog blast-radius). | **KILL CRITERION, STATED BEFORE THE READ: if a MAJORITY of post-deploy learner publications remain UNSHELVED (zero join rows AND null `course_program`), the un-shadowing did not transfer authority — it removed representation. Remedy is a PUBLISH-TIME classification step, NOT reverting the clearing**, which would restore the curator-authority defect the release closed. **⚠️ Q3 IS WHAT MAKES Q1 INTERPRETABLE and must not be skipped:** learner-authored public notes were never shadowed, so their classification rate is the baseline — a low Q1 matched by an equally low Q3 means the finding is about **the field**, not this release, and must not be attributed to the clearing. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE, BINDING: ZERO learner copies were public on 2026-09-05, so this cohort starts EMPTY.** Too small to read is **itself the finding** — it would mean learners essentially never publish copies, making the closed hazard more theoretical than 536 rows suggested. **Record that and CLOSE; do not silently re-date.** | 2026-09-05 |

Slices 0A/0B remain owner-executed and unstarted; Slices 4-5 (structural updates) and the Companion are DEFERRED, and `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-05]`'s kill criterion (a) gates whether they are built at all.** ⚠️ SLICE 1 SHIPPED AS `v0.115.0` (2026-09-05) — this row no longer describes unstarted work.** Slices 0A/0B are owner-executed and unstarted; **Slice 2 is the next engineering slice and is NOT started.** **⚠️ SLICE 1's VERIFICATION PRODUCED A CARRIED LESSON FOR EVERY LATER SLICE: a bare `role == ADMIN` substitution for `CuratorAuthoringPredicate.isCurator` SURVIVED all 99 tests in `NoteServiceTest`** while it would have stripped authored applicability off every TEACHER-owned curated note — because the ADMIN fixture and the learner fixture between them satisfied both guards without exercising the TEACHER leg. **Every later slice touching the curator predicate must pin BOTH legs.** | **⚠️ THE AUDIT FALSIFIED SIX PREMISES OF THE BRIEF — read §1 before acting on any of it:** the taxonomy has **11 values not 8** (`v0.111.0` shipped three), the catalog holds **44 programs not 21**, the ALE residue is **88 rows not 215**, and **ZERO notes are in the multi-program blocked state** (the exception has zero occupancy, so that blocker is prospective not live). **⚠️ THE HEADLINE: all three workstreams need LESS architecture than the brief anticipates.** Domain Context's real defect is **classification COVERAGE, not vocabulary** — the generation gate, not curator diligence, drives classification (699 of 809 single-program notes unclassified), which is the evidence-based explanation for all four zero-usage values and for **~239 curator notes generating with NO computation guidance on the permanent-per-note path**. Note copy: **both options D and C are REFUTED** (C fires on zero notes; D is refuted by 608 null-string / 551 multi-row source notes) — the fix is one write path at the publish transition. Adoption: **the update mechanism ALREADY EXISTS and is idempotent** (`copyNote` is idempotent per `(owner, copied_from_note_id)`), so what is missing is the decision to call it plus three provenance facts. **⚠️ SEQUENCING IS A CORRECTNESS CONSTRAINT, NOT A PREFERENCE: the publish-transition fix MUST precede the first update pass** — measured 2026-09-05, **100% of adopted placements' source notes carry join rows** while only 11.8% of existing copies do, so an update pass run today grows the hazard population by ~8x in one batch. **⚠️ REVISED 2026-09-05 AFTER OWNER/PRODUCT-UX TIGHTENING — four corrections, all to errors this session introduced: (1) any EXPECTED RESIDUE FIGURE for the ALE pass is REMOVED — publishing one before the pass runs is the exact failure that contaminated the original 215 rows, so the honest residue is an OUTPUT, never a target; (2) classification is TREATMENT-FIRST — `ADR-001` decides the value and `quantitative` is a CONSEQUENCE, so do NOT classify a Nursing or Accountancy note to switch on computation guidance; (3) "the numerator never shrinks" is WITHDRAWN as too broad — the invariant is "learner study history is preserved", since current-curriculum counts may legitimately change on retirement; (4) AI-tooling scheduling is removed from the architecture doc entirely.** **⚠️ PLACEMENT IDENTITY IS NOW SETTLED FROM THE DATABASE, NOT INFERRED: `note_collection_items` carries `UNIQUE (collection_id, note_id)`, so durable placement identity IS `(source Subject Plan, source Note)` — no new column, no title matching. A note CANNOT appear twice in one Subject Plan or in two Sections of it, and CAN appear in several Subject Plans.** **⚠️ Idempotence is therefore SETTLED AT THE STORAGE LAYER AND STILL OPEN AT THE APPLICATION LAYER** — whether the insert path handles the unique violation gracefully is unverified and needs a repeated-application regression test before signoff. **SEQUENCE: 0A (ALE `domain_context` pass) · 0B (broader classification: Nursing, Accountancy, Architecture) · 1 (publish-transition applicability) · 2 (additive updates + migration) · 3 (early-adopter rollout) · 4-5 (structural updates, Companion — deferred).** **⚠️ Slices 0A/0B are OWNER-EXECUTED curator work writing `domain_context` on production rows — they need a `docs/claude-plans/*.sql` handoff plus BEFORE/AFTER capture, and the ALE build is gated on the after-capture. They are INDEPENDENT of Slices 1-2 and do NOT hold early-adopter work behind taxonomy expansion.** | 2026-09-05 |

That **refutes** a database-side stall and an anonymous read burst, and leaves **(a)** holds across slow external calls **or (b)** an unconsidered **connection LEAK** — which Phase 3 does nothing for. **See §11 of the finding; `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-04]` decides between them.** **⚠️ AMENDED 2026-09-04 (`v0.114.0`) — §5 WAS FINALLY RUN AND §11's REFUTATION OF THE READ BURST IS WITHDRAWN. So `select count(*) from linked_learner_provisional_birth_years` **stops being the whole answer the moment expiry is live: a swept pile reads as an empty one.** **⚠️ RE-SPECIFIED READ:** count relationships in `PENDING` **plus** `EXPIRED` created before the read date, and count them **separately** — `PENDING` is the accumulation this row was written to detect and `EXPIRED` is the accumulation the remedy already absorbed. **⚠️ THE DENOMINATOR WIDENS, AND THAT MUST BE STATED RATHER THAN GLOSSED: the sum is NOT what the original `COUNT(*)` would have returned.** The original counts **provisional rows**, which exist only where a **link redemption declared a year**; a `PENDING` relationship from the **email-keyed path has no provisional row at all**. So restrict the re-specified read to relationships that **had** a provisional row — recoverable as `PENDING` rows with a live provisional row, plus `EXPIRED` rows, since expiry is the only transition that deletes one without promoting or revoking it — **or**, if that reconstruction is not clean at read time, report the wider relationship count **explicitly labelled as a different denominator**. **The comparison this row exists to make is accumulation-over-time, and a silently widened denominator would read as growth that never happened.** A high `EXPIRED` count is **not** a pass; it is the same finding with the remedy already applied, and it confirms the kill criterion fired correctly rather than refuting it. **⚠️ THE ARITHMETIC PROTECTS THIS READ AND IS THE REASON ITEM 1 WAS ALLOWED TO SHIP FIRST:** the earliest provisional row dates from the `v0.95.0` deploy (2026-08-29), so a **≥30-day TTL dated from relationship `created_at`** makes the earliest possible expiry **2026-09-28 — after this read**. If that TTL bound or clock is ever changed, **this row must be re-dated before the change ships.** |

| **⚠️ SCOPED AS `v0.100.0` — Domain Context resolver + UX: 13 ratified product decisions, Stage 2** | Product-UX consultation, 2026-08-31, responding to the Stage 1 cold audit. Decisions and the three code-verified conflicts: `docs/claude-plans/domain-context-taxonomy-calibration-audit.md` (Stage 2 section). | **SCOPED — kicked off as `v0.100.0` on 2026-09-01, five items. The taxonomy questions are ACCEPTED AS SETTLED.** No `ARCHITECTURE`, no catch-all, no removals, no multi-valued Domain Context, no program list to the LLM. **The work is resolver + UX + mental model.** Headline decisions: replace `Automatic — based on the program` with **`Automatic — use note context`**; **show the EFFECTIVE writing domain** as derived state, never a new persisted field and never a frontend re-implementation of resolver precedence; **canonical curated generation must not silently inherit the curator's profile program**; Domain Context stays **optional**; **block ambiguity at GENERATION, not at note save**. | **⚠️ THREE CONFLICTS FOUND AGAINST REAL CODE — read them before implementing.** **(1) Decisions 1 and 4 are COUPLED:** *"use note context"* is only truthful once profile fallback is removed for curated generation, so shipping the copy alone makes the label wrong again in a new way. **(2) Decision 6 COLLIDES with an existing save-time block** — `assertMultiProgramHasDomainContext` already hard-blocks a 2+ program note with no Domain Context, in four write paths. Either that is an explicit exception (defensible — the resolver genuinely cannot choose between programs) or it moves to generation time, which is a larger change to an existing error contract. **⚠️ Needs an owner ruling; do not decide it in implementation.** **(3) Decision 4's boundary ALREADY EXISTS** — `isTeacherSelectableOwner`/`isCurator` is `TEACHER OR ADMIN` behind an onboarding guard and is **already used for this exact field** (`NoteService:182` nulls a curator note's personal program), so no brittle role check needs inventing and the `ADMIN`-alone concern is moot. **⚠️ Decision 11 (override behaviour) is NOT reconstructable from current data** — Domain Context authoring emits zero analytics and nothing records what Automatic would have produced. Patterns A/C/D are partly answerable by the existing Q5 query; **Pattern B and Decision 11 need instrumentation that must exist BEFORE the behaviour occurs**, and the September read is 28 days out. **⚠️ DECISION 6 RULED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-01 — AND THE RULING TOOK THE LARGER OPTION: the existing save-time block MOVES to generation time**, rather than being carved out as a data-integrity exception. One uniform rule, *note validity ≠ generation readiness*. **⚠️ THAT RULING RELAXES A SAVE-TIME INVARIANT AND CREATES A PRODUCTION DATA STATE THAT HAS NEVER EXISTED** — a note with 2+ Applicable Programs and a NULL Domain Context — so **Q6 of this document's own read changes meaning**: a non-zero result means "a write path regressed, a live bug" today, and "legacy or legal" afterwards. **That interpretation must be updated in the same release or the September read will be misread.** **⚠️ THE A–F IMPLEMENTATION AUDIT RAN 2026-09-01 — `docs/claude-plans/v0.100.0-domain-context-implementation-audit.md`** (this row is its index entry). **It re-shaped the release and added a sixth item.** Findings: only three save-time sites move (two are already generation-time); **the primary generation path validates nothing**, so a naive item 3 deletes the invariant instead of moving it, silently; **Decisions 4 and 6 are coupled** as well as 1 and 4; item 2 **shrinks** because `effectiveAuthoringDomain` already exists as a public static shared with the prompt builder; item 4 was **insufficient**, since no `NOTE_UPDATED` event exists and both Domain-Context-editing surfaces are update paths; the curator predicate is **private or inlined in all four copies**, so a fifth copy must not be added; and a claimed **second leak on the reader axis** was found by enumerating all 18 resolver call sites and then **RETRACTED the same day** — enumerating identities is not the same as checking reachability, and a non-owned pack cannot reach the resolver. **Item 6 is reduced to a regression guard pinning the three owner-passing sites.** The retraction is kept at full strength in the audit; a deleted wrong claim teaches nothing. **⚠️ TIER RE-EVALUATED RATHER THAN INHERITED:** the audit set *"one scoped cold agent at minimum; re-evaluate if the Decision 6 ruling widens to moving the existing save-block"* — **it did widen** — and the re-evaluation still lands on **one scoped cold agent, framed as falsification**, because the full three-agent test is reserved for a permission substrate or a first-of-kind cross-user read and this release has neither. It fires the one-agent trigger three times over: production-data semantics, a changed error contract across four write paths, and a change to what reaches the LLM. **Escalate if the A–F audit finds item 1's curator boundary is not cleanly separable from the learner path, or if item 3's four write paths turn out not to be four.** | 2026-09-01 |

Interview Practice is genuinely multi-source (`InterviewSourceNoteRef`, `additionalNoteIds`), so it over-attributes on every multi-source session **today, and did so before `v0.104.0` existed.** **⚠️ IT PARTIALLY UNDERCUTS `v0.104.0`'s OWN PURPOSE:** the audit's §32 goal reads `ConceptHealth` written by **all** modes, so subject-level reporting stays contaminated — Long Exam and Board Exam are now trustworthy, the store as a whole is not. **⚠️ Deferred for RELEASE SIZE, not because it is blocked:** fixing it needs the same two coupled changes in a third service (stamp its generation path, then aggregate per source), and folding a third service into a release that already changed what production evidence means was judged the worse trade. | **~~[DEFECT]~~ ✅ RESOLVED `v0.107.0`.** Stamped at the generation seam, aggregated per source, `resolveSourceStudyPackIds` demoted to an unstamped-item fallback. **⚠️ Rows written BEFORE the deploy remain over-attributed — no backfill, matching `v0.104.0`'s precedent.** Originally: **⚠️ SCOPED 2026-09-03 after THREE releases of deferral**, folded with slice 5 because slice 5 exists only to consume the evidence this defect corrupts. **⚠️ RE-VERIFIED BY READING CODE AT THAT KICKOFF, AND THE ROW UNDERSTATED HOW CHEAP IT IS — plausibly why it was deferred three times:** `QuizSessionReviewUtils.computeKeyConceptBreakdownBySourceStudyPack` (`:173`) **already exists** and is already used by `LongExamService:508` with identical `keyConcept` semantics, so the fix needs **no new util, no new API, no DTO change and no migration** — one stamp at the `generateQuizForSources:573-604` merge seam plus a near-verbatim port of `LongExamService:550-600`. **⚠️ `resolveSourceStudyPackIds` MUST BE DEMOTED TO FALLBACK-ONLY; left ambiguous, aggregation AND broadcast both run, which is WORSE THAN TODAY and the obvious fixture does not catch it.** **⚠️ AND A CORRECTNESS TRAP THE MULTI-PACK FIXTURE CANNOT CATCH:** Interview Practice calls the **3-arg** `computeFullyCorrectKeyConcepts(quiz, selectedChoices, Map.of())` where that `Map.of()` is `selectedMultiChoices`, while the breakdown helper takes **5** — a wrong mapping silently changes **what counts as correct** while appearing to change only attribution, and both packs move together. **Pre-declared guard: a SINGLE-SOURCE session must write byte-identical `ConceptHealth` before and after.** **⚠️ THE FIX IS PROSPECTIVE — `v0.104.0` shipped NO backfill, so already-written contaminated rows stay contaminated and slice 5 reads them.** | 2026-09-03 |

"wire it in".** **⚠️ AMENDED AT THE `v0.104.0` KICKOFF, 2026-09-02 — THE AUDIT'S §5 "MINIMUM FIX" IS INCOMPLETE AS WRITTEN AND MUST NOT BE IMPLEMENTED LITERALLY.** §5 says *"attach the source Study Pack id to each generated item"* and stops there. **The stamp is necessary and NOT sufficient:** `QuizSessionReviewUtils.computeConceptBreakdown:85` keys its counters by `normalizeConcept(item.concept())` **alone**, collapsing every source into one map **downstream of the stamp**, so the correct/missed lists are already merged before `ConceptHealth` is written — one in both directions.** Slice 1 is therefore **two coupled changes**: provenance on the item **and** per-source aggregation on the write path, bucketing by `(sourceStudyPackId, concept)`. **⚠️ AND THE AGGREGATION HAS AN INDEX TRAP:** `computeConceptBreakdown` passes a **positional** index into `isAnswerCorrect` while the selection maps are keyed by **absolute index in the session quiz array**, so filtering to one pack's items and reusing the helper reads the **wrong learner answers**. **⚠️ THE SECOND INSTANCE IS ALSO RECORDED: `ChallengeQuizService.completeSession:674,680` UNDER-attributes to the primary pack, and that path is NEW as of `v0.103.0`** — the audit's original text describes `LongExamService` alone. **⚠️ Provenance goes on `QuizItem`, never a parallel per-index array — `ChallengeQuizService:391` shuffles the merged quiz after the merge and `:522` re-shuffles the redo round, so an index-keyed array is scrambled by a shuffle that already ships.** **⚠️ ONE BLOCKING DEFECT: ConceptHealth provenance is broken for multi-source exams** — the same concept list is written to EVERY source pack and `QuizItem` has no source-pack field, so per-subject strength reporting repo-answered, and it de-risks the Board Exam slice. **Verdict: EVOLVE, not rebuild** — seven slices across at least four releases, of which slices 2, 3 and 6 each independently meet the pre-signoff gate. | **⚠️ ARCHITECTURE AND SEQUENCE APPROVED 2026-09-02; ALL FOUR OWNER DECISIONS ANSWERED.** **(1) The Plus cap of 4 is REJECTED as arithmetic leakage** (`12/3`), not product design — preserve a materially larger custom mixed-retrieval experience, exact figure tunable. **(2) Board Exam's question count is CONFIGURATION**, never a permanent universal constant. **(3) Partial generation yields a shorter exam ONLY above a defined minimum assembly/coverage threshold; otherwise it fails WITHOUT consuming quota** — never silently equate a severely incomplete exam with a complete one. **(4) The row is owed **in the signoff commit of whichever release ships the prompt change**, per the signoff gate — kickoff step 9 only scans for *overdue* checkpoints and structurally cannot catch one never written. **⚠️ NO automated rename, no post-processing, no suffix stripping, no bulk migration** — the report is a decision aid only. **ANTI-DRIFT:** titles describe knowledge, not curriculum ownership; a title must not duplicate or compensate for Subject, Domain Context, Authored Depth, Applicable Programs or Review Set placement; **a program in Applicable Programs does NOT mean it belongs in the title**, and a discipline in the title does NOT imply exclusive ownership; never infer metadata from title wording or vice versa; **do not rename learner-owned notes, do not propagate curated title changes into learner copies, and do not validate against a learner naming a note "Fluid Mechanics for my CE Finals"**; fix generation at the **prompt** level, not with code; and do not duplicate shared knowledge into program-specific notes merely to obtain program-specific titles. **Governing principle, verbatim: *Name the knowledge, not the curriculum container. Curriculum-neutral where possible. Knowledge-specific always.*** **Migration principle: *fix future generation now; normalize legacy titles when they undergo meaningful review.*** |

So an unconfirmed request sits indefinitely, holding the redeemer's declared birth year. **Bounded in practice rather than unbounded:** either party can revoke (deliberately ungated), the request is visible in the redeemer's own connections list, and account deletion cascades it away. **⚠️ Sequencing matters** — `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-13]` reads invitation TTL against acceptance rates, so changing what expires touches its window. **⚠️ AND THE SHARPER COLLISION WAS MISSING FROM THIS ROW UNTIL THE `v0.97.0` KICKOFF, 2026-08-29: `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-26]` (`v0.95.0`) names *"expiry for unconfirmed requests"* as its OWN prescribed response, and its metric is a `COUNT(*)` on `linked_learner_provisional_birth_years` — exactly the rows a sweep deletes.** So building this ships the remedy ahead of the diagnosis *and* deletes the diagnosis. **⚠️ IT CLEARS ONLY BY ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS WHY THE TTL IS A CONSTRAINT AND NOT A DEFAULT:** the provisional table exists only from the `v0.95.0` deploy (2026-08-29), so a **TTL of ≥30 days dated from relationship `created_at`** puts the earliest possible expiry at **2026-09-28, after the read** — the same reasoning `2026-10-13` used. Part (c) measures that directly. **Instrumentation:** no analytics event is involved and none is needed — the read keys on the two clock columns `V118` added, written by five verified call sites (four pool status writes, one note enqueue), so the metric is the table itself rather than an event that might not fire. **⚠️ EARLY READ RUN 2026-08-19 — the historical backlog DRAINED, which is NOT the same as the kill criterion passing, and the denominator clause above is what makes the difference.** Step 6a returned `FAILED/BOARD_EXAM 40` and `FAILED/LONG_EXAM 36` and **nothing else** — the query lists every non-`READY` status, so `GENERATING` = **0** and `PENDING` = **0**, down from the 37 that were stuck. That transition could not have come from normal use: recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act while a row still reads `PENDING`/`GENERATING`, which was the defect. The 76 `FAILED` pools now self-heal through `refreshPool` on next use, which is the designed end state. **⚠️ But this is precisely the case the denominator clause anticipates:** it shows the sweeper fired at least once against a known backlog; it does **not** show it will catch a future stranding, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. **Part (c) still decides the verdict on 2026-09-01 — do not close this row on the early read.** **⚠️ Step 6a CANNOT attribute the change to the sweeper, and must not be quoted as if it does.** It selects `min/max(created_at)`, the clock `v0.86.0` itself declared unusable for pools because rows are reused — which is precisely why `V118` added `generation_status_at`. The `2026-07-03` timestamps therefore mean only *no new pool row has been inserted since then*, and a later reader could easily misread them as evidence the sweeper never fired. **Attribution query owed, one line:** `select generation_status, count(*), count(*) filter (where generation_status_at is null) as unstamped, min(generation_status_at), max(generation_status_at) from exam_question_pool where generation_status <> 'READY' group by 1;` — roughly 37 rows stamped at or after the `v0.86.0` deploy confirms the sweeper; all-`NULL` or all-pre-deploy means something else cleared them and the sweeper is still inert. **⚠️ Step 6b is still UNRUN, and it covers the more severe half** — the 1 Long Exam session stuck since 2026-05-19 was a *hard permanent block* (`LongExamService:169` hands the learner back the stuck session rather than creating one), where the pool damage only degraded exam starts to on-demand generation. **✅ VERDICT 2026-08-19 — PASS on the primary question, closed 18 days early.** Read (a) returned **0/0/0/0**: nothing past its bound on any of the four surfaces. Read (d) is what makes this a verdict rather than an absence: **76 FAILED pools, 39 unstamped, 37 stamped** — and **37 is exactly the stuck population** (18 `GENERATING` + 19 `PENDING`). `V118` seeded `generation_status_at` only for non-terminal rows, so those 37 *are* the previously-stuck set, and they are now all FAILED. Nothing but the sweeper transitions non-terminal → FAILED by age; recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act on a non-terminal row, which was the whole defect. **This is what resolves the denominator clause** — there WAS a backlog, it was 37 rows, and it drained. Read (e) confirms the severe half: **the `LONG_EXAM` session stranded since 2026-05-19 is now `FAILED`**, so the hard permanent block is cleared. (The `IN_PROGRESS`/`LONG_EXAM` row from 2026-05-15 is an abandoned session, not a stuck one — `IN_PROGRESS` is deliberately not swept.) **⚠️ Residual, stated honestly: this proves the sweeper drained a KNOWN backlog once. It does not prove it catches a future stranding**, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. Three shapes exist: commit the session before the bank write, insert unclaimed rows in the isolated transaction and claim them in the outer one, or drop the session FKs. **They differ in failure semantics, not just mechanics** — the second preserves provenance, the third re-opens the orphan class `V116` exists to clean up. **Gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-15]`:** if the remaining concurrent-duplicate case never shows up in logs, this may not be worth building at all | 2026-08-16 |

| **~~No test ever executes a native query against PostgreSQL~~ — RESOLVED in `v0.93.0`, and it found TWO MORE production defects on its first run** | Found by a cold-context agent at the `v0.92.0` signoff, 2026-08-27, which `PREPARE`d all 31 native queries against a real PostgreSQL 16 carrying the full 125-migration schema | **RESOLVED 2026-08-28 (`v0.93.0` item 7).** `NativeQueryPostgresIntegrationTest` ships: one PostgreSQL 16 Testcontainers context applying all 125 Flyway migrations, a reflective `PREPARE` sweep over all 31 annotated native queries asserted at an exact count, execution of both PostgreSQL-only Library implementations, and assertions on the PG-only tag filter. Docker is required by default; the only opt-out is an explicit `-Dnativequery.pg.skip=true`, which warns. **⚠️ On its FIRST run it found two live production defects** — `wherelower(...)` from a Java text block stripping the trailing space before its closing delimiter, and `:learnerLeveland` from the next fragment starting at `and` — both PostgreSQL-only, both dating to `7f69f3d8` (2026-07-19), so **Public Library tag filtering had been broken in production for ~40 days**. **⚠️ Do NOT read this as total coverage:** `PREPARE` validates syntax, types and `ON CONFLICT` arbiter resolution and **never predicate correctness** — a cold agent mutated the `v0.93.0` conditional insert and the sweep passed it happily, which is why that statement now has its own executing test. `findNativeQueries` scans only `com.studysnap.backend.repository.**` via `getDeclaredMethods()`, and `appendSourceFilter`'s anonymous `viewerUserId == null` branches emit SQL the fixtures never produce. `NativeQueryParameterTypingTest` is retained as the Docker-free tripwire. Original analysis follows. **OPEN — this was the durable fix for the defect class that produced `v0.83.1` and the `v0.91.0` *Shared with you* 500.** **⚠️ Three structural facts, all verified: (1)** `src/test/resources/application.yaml` sets `flyway.enabled: false` and `ddl-auto: none`, so the **125 migrations never run in tests** — integration tests hand-write minimal H2 DDL per class (one creates `users` with TWO columns), which makes a `NOT NULL` column with no default **structurally undetectable**; that is `v0.83.1`'s root cause, still open. **(2)** `NoteLibraryRepositoryImpl` and `PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl` branch on `isPostgres()` at runtime, so **~20 dynamic queries have never executed on any database in any test** — their PG-only branches (`unnest`, `&&` array overlap, `cross join lateral`, `jsonb_array_length`) are unreachable by source-text or reflection because those strings exist only when assembled at runtime. **(3) There is no `.github/workflows` at all**, so every guard is honour-system on the local `./mvnw clean install`. **✅ Good news, empirically established: all 31 native queries currently PASS** — zero remaining instances of the class. `NativeQueryParameterTypingTest` was hardened at this signoff after the same agent proved it missed `:param is not null` (the identical failure, reintroduced and shipped green). **⚠️ Only TWO shapes actually fail on PG16** — a parameter whose sole type context is `IS [NOT] NULL`, and arithmetic on a bare parameter; `coalesce`, `nullif`, `case`, `lower()`, `$1 = $2`, `order by $1` and `limit $1` all prepare cleanly. **Do NOT lengthen the guard's pattern beyond those two** — that restores the guess `v0.85.0` argued against. **⚠️ Typing is ORDER-SENSITIVE:** `(col < :p or :p is null)` prepares while `(:p is null or col < :p)` does not, so "just reorder the OR" is a landmine a later readability edit re-arms — keep the casts | **Recommended and costed: `org.testcontainers:postgresql` + `junit-jupiter` (test scope) with `@ServiceConnection` and Flyway ENABLED so the real migrations build the schema**, carrying (a) a reflection-driven `PREPARE` sweep over every `@Query(nativeQuery=true)` — 31 queries, zero hand-maintenance, and the extraction pattern already exists in `LinkedLearnerInvitationReArmTest` — and (b) one integration test exercising every Library/PublicLibrary filter, the only thing that can reach the ~20 dynamic queries by forcing `isPostgres()` true. Cost: one test-scoped dependency, Docker at test time (already the documented dev loop), ~15–30s container start. **⚠️ The cheaper "PREPARE only when a PG is reachable" option was considered and rejected as false comfort** — with no CI it is honour-system either way, and it covers strictly less; enabling Flyway on real PostgreSQL is the half that closes `v0.83.1`'s class too | none — shipped | 2026-08-28 |

| **Support Another Learner — helping someone else learn, as a capability rather than a Profile Type** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14, revising a same-day "Guardian profile" draft that modelled it as a new `ProfileType`. Proposal: `docs/claude-plans/support-another-learner-proposal.md`. Surfacing ratification: `docs/claude-plans/learning-companion-surfacing-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md` (written and answered 2026-08-20 — **a standing decision a future re-proposal must read, not a spent artifact**) | **Direction ratified, scope NOT ratified.** The job is *help someone else learn* — a parent, grandparent, sibling, tutor, mentor or coach — not one relationship. **`ProfileType` answers "how do YOU learn?"; supporting someone answers "who else are you helping?"** Those stay independent axes, the same correction `ADR-001` made for `course_program`; a supporter may therefore also be a learner, which an enum value forbids. **Ratified rollout is three phases:** (1) help another learner — no shared subscription, no shared quota, no dashboards, **no permissions model**; (2) linked learners, invitation and acceptance; (3) progress sharing. **Phase 3 is where the product's first cross-user read lives**, so Phases 1–2 ship without touching authorization at all. **Ratified privacy line: a supporter sees readiness, progress and quiz performance, NEVER the learner's personal notes** — notes are personal thinking, progress is educational, and the line also protects the core loop, since learners who suspect notes are visible write less honestly. **Ratified: no per-learner charge** — the link is metadata and costs nothing; each learner keeps their own account and quota | **✅ SURFACING RATIFIED 2026-08-20, after a product-UX consultation was written and the owner answered it directly.** **Option A (an opt-in toggle) REJECTED — Learning Connections stays a CAPABILITY, never a profile mode or an opt-in mode**; a preference would re-introduce the very mode the capability framing exists to remove. **"Quiz for someone" leaves the Study Pack practice row entirely** — it is a support/share action, not learner practice, and does not belong beside Start Quick Review, where it also risks becoming an avoidance path on the surface retention is decided. It moves to a secondary **note action / share** surface. **⚠️ CORRECTION TO THE SESSION'S OWN PROPOSAL: quiz sharing is NOT gated on having an accepted connection.** That option coupled two independent things — **a shared-quiz recipient needs no account and no relationship**, so sharing cannot depend on one existing. **Accepted connections gate supporter-specific surfaces ONLY** (People You Support, progress); they never determine whether sharing exists. **Learning Connections stays independently discoverable**, so someone with no relationship can still learn about both. **`/progress` stays focused on the CURRENT learner**; supporter progress stays under Learning Connections / Dashboard, because the two carry different privacy contracts. **⚠️ Connection invitations stay ONE-AT-A-TIME as a principle, not a deferral: sharing a quiz link IS the many-recipient mechanism, while forming an ongoing, permission-bearing relationship is personal and should stay deliberate.** The email-keyed-invitation work still stands on its own merits — it closes the account-existence oracle (**SHIPPED in `v0.90.0`**, along with invitation expiry, two-key rate limiting on the invite endpoint, and a verified-email gate on every path that grants or widens access; relationship rows are now created only at acceptance, which changed what `PENDING` means — see the `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]` row, whose read this alters) regardless of whether multi-recipient invites are ever built. **✅ Q4 AND Q5 ALSO RATIFIED 2026-08-19, which unblocks Phase 2 scoping.** **Q4 — linking is invite + accept in both directions, revocable from either side.** Acceptance is the load-bearing half: an opt-out notification would leave the relationship existing, and progress potentially visible, before the learner agreed to anything. Learner-initiated-code-only was rejected — strictest, but it requires the learner to act first, failing exactly where the parent→young-child case needs it most. **Q5 — age is collected AT LINK TIME, never at signup, with a guardian consent step below the threshold.** Collecting at link time keeps onboarding untouched, which matters concretely because `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]` is measuring it against a 62.4% baseline. **⚠️ `learnerLevel` was considered as an age proxy and REJECTED** — it implies minors for `GRADE_SCHOOL`/`JUNIOR_HIGH`/`SENIOR_HIGH` and costs nothing, but the mapping breaks both ways (`PERSONAL_LEARNING`, adult re-takers) and a proxy is weaker ground than a declared age. Adults-only-first was rejected because it excludes the motivating parent→child case. **⚠️ THE AGE THRESHOLD ITSELF IS NOT DECIDED AND MUST NOT BE GUESSED IN SCOPING** — the mechanism is ratified, the number is a legal question under the Philippine Data Privacy Act and is owner-owned pending counsel. Build the threshold as configuration, not a literal, and ship no default that reads as a legal position. **✅ THE DECISION GATE IS CLEARED — audit run 2026-08-19, and Phase 1 SCOPED INTO `v0.89.0`.** The answer to *does Phase 1 already exist* is **yes, almost entirely**: generating a quiz for another person is ungated, `/quiz/share/**` is `permitAll` so the recipient needs no account, and plan metering already exists. **One gate hides all of it — `QuizShareLinkService.requireTeacherOrAdmin:160-166`** — and it is an axis error, not a missing feature: a parent helping a child is a `STUDENT`/`BOARD_EXAM` profile and must misrepresent their own profile to unlock it. **⚠️ The audit also DISSOLVED three of this row's other blockers for Phase 1 specifically**, because Phase 1 records no relationship: consent has nothing to consent to, minors/DPA store nothing about the recipient, and *whose quota* is already answered by the existing code (the supporter's own account and own share limit). **They all return from Phase 2**, when a relationship is recorded. Remaining original gate text follows. **`[DECISION]` + `[EVIDENCE]`.** **Decision first: does Phase 1 already largely exist?** Teachers already generate quizzes for others via `generatedQuiz`, and shareable quiz links already work — so Phase 1 may be a **packaging and discoverability** problem rather than a build. Audit that before scoping. **Also undecided: whose quota a supporter-initiated generation consumes** (the link is free, but generating costs tokens — recommendation is the supporter's own, which dissolves the pricing question entirely), the consent model for linking (**must be mutual, or anyone could claim a relationship by knowing an email**), and minors/Data-Privacy-Act obligations, since age is not collected today. **Evidence: zero users on the unimplemented `PARENT` enum value.** A demand signal is designed in §9 of the proposal — **and it must NOT go in onboarding before 2026-09-11**, which is under measurement against a 62.4% baseline. **Do not justify Phase 1 on retention:** the supporter's return loop is the progress view, which is Phase 3 | 2026-08-14 |
| **Adaptive Practice becomes the recommendation engine, not a post-quiz next step** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14. Full direction, findings and open questions: `docs/claude-plans/adaptive-practice-recommendation-engine.md` | **FIRST SLICE SHIPPED as `v0.77.0` (2026-08-14):** the Dashboard's weak-concept focus now resolves from `ConceptHealth`'s `incorrect_streak` against the existing `TWICE_MISSED_STREAK_THRESHOLD` instead of the single latest completed Quick Review, so one bad quiz no longer produces a recommendation. **Within-pack only** — cross-pack still needs canonical concept identity. **No entry point was removed**, so the 2026-09-12 constraint is untouched. Philosophy ratified, remaining scope NOT ratified. Adaptive Practice should appear because a learner has demonstrated a **persistent** weakness across multiple assessments — not because they just finished a quiz. Primarily **dashboard-driven** (Today's Focus / Weak Concepts), with Challenge Quiz completion celebrating the quiz instead of offering remediation. Target feeling: *"the system noticed something I keep struggling with"*, not *"the system wants me to take another quiz"*. **This is the first mechanism that would make "Always know what to learn next" literally true.** Smaller than it sounds in one respect: the Dashboard already routes to Adaptive Practice from its due- and weak-concept branches, so the change is **raising the evidence bar and making it the primary route**, not adding a surface | **`[DECISION]` — and it carries a HARD SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT.** ⚠️ **Do not remove the Challenge Quiz entry point before 2026-09-12.** `v0.74.0` already removed Adaptive Practice from the Quick Review result screen, and the open `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]` measures exactly that; a second removal confounds the read rather than answering it. **Worse, that checkpoint's kill criterion says to "restore a demoted entry point" if adoption falls — which directly contradicts this direction**, so the checkpoint ~~must be re-specified now (remedy becomes *accelerate the dashboard recommendation*), or it will fire in September and someone will correctly follow an instruction that undoes a ratified direction.~~ **✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-14 — the remedy WAS re-specified, and the checkpoint row carries it struck through. ⚠️ THIS WARNING IS STALE AND IS STRUCK THROUGH RATHER THAN DELETED, because it caused a REAL wrong scoping decision at the `v0.107.0` kickoff (2026-09-03), which scoped an already-completed re-specification as new work.** Two rows described one obligation — this one carried the warning, the checkpoint row carried the completed action — and the warning row was read first. **A future reader needs the discharge visible HERE, on the row that misled, not only on the row that was right.** **Architecture finding: the missing layer is CONCEPT IDENTITY, not just a learning signal.** `ConceptHealthEntity` already stores `incorrect_streak`, so within-pack repeated evidence is **available today** — but `concept` is a free-text string keyed per Study Pack, so cross-pack recommendation (*"this keeps coming back everywhere"*) needs canonical concepts first, the same problem `ADR-001` solved for programs. **Within-pack and cross-pack differ by roughly an order of magnitude in cost; decide which is meant first.** **Second fork:** this either justifies consolidating the **8 independent "what's next" resolvers** the Companion Guidance Doctrine pressure test found, or becomes the 9th — and that doctrine's blocking Phase 0 (Primary-Review-Set vs Study/Exam-Focus) probably cannot be dodged by a dashboard-first engine. **Product tension:** Adaptive Practice is capped at 3/month on Free, so a higher evidence bar may mean the tier most likely to convert never sees a recommendation at all | 2026-08-14 |
| **Onboarding Intent Router — onboarding as an intelligent router, not profile collection** | `v0.71.0` pre-signoff pressure test (`docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`), which found onboarding the flow most affected by ADR-001 Release B. Full plan: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md`. Production audit queries: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit.sql` | **Scoped into `v0.71.0` as slice 5 — owner ruling 2026-08-06.** Added after kickoff, deliberately.
