| Companion Guidance Doctrine (Ask Companion → cross-cutting "Companion" system, considered and narrowed) | Owner + GPT discussion, pressure-tested with Opus, 2026-07-29, same conversation as `v0.64.0`'s Companion-scoping question | **Considered and narrowed, not rejected outright.** Proposal: turn "Ask Companion" into a cross-cutting guidance system powering Dashboard/Review Set/Progress/Readiness with one deterministic-first voice, LLM only on explicit request. Pressure test found the *taxonomy* ("one learning responsibility per feature") sound but the *literal system-merge* too wide — "Companion" today names three structurally different things (admin-authored static content, learner-reactive derived guidance i.e. "Coach," and the LLM chat), and merging the name removes the vocabulary keeping them safely apart (a learner would see "Companion" guidance on Dashboard, then be told it's unavailable on a Review Set with no admin-authored content). Also found 8 existing, independent "what's next" resolvers already exist across Dashboard/Collection detail/Progress (each with documented reasons for diverging, e.g. Dashboard/collection pacing must stay uncoupled per `docs/features/dashboard.md:95`) sitting on only 2 shared signal primitives — a real, costly merge target, not free. **Resolution: no rename, no new user-facing brand, no backend merge now.** Adopt "one learning responsibility per feature" + "one question per surface" + a de-duplication rule as an authoring *doctrine* (docs/copy only); keep `Feature.ASK_COMPANION`/`ask_companion_sessions`/`AnalyticsEventType.ASK_COMPANION_*` unchanged. Phased plan if this is ever scoped: Phase 0 resolve Primary-vs-Focus (blocking) → Phase 1 doctrine + copy audit (cheap, no backend) → Phase 2 additive-only structured `reason` field on existing resolvers (substrate, no merge) → Phase 3 lightweight one-off "why this?" LLM explainer (needs a new message-unit mechanism — cannot reuse `ask_companion_sessions`'s session-unit quota/DB-CHECK turn cap) → Phase 4 Personalization (unchanged). Merge the 8 resolvers only if Phase 1 empirically proves the ladder is identical across surfaces — explicit go/no-go criterion, recorded here so it isn't re-litigated. Also surfaced a preventive gap: `EXAM_MODES.md`'s locked no-mid-exam-coaching rule never contemplated a persistent cross-cutting guidance presence and says nothing about an exam launch/confirm screen specifically — worth an explicit line whenever Phase 1 is scoped. `v0.64.0`'s already-planned scope (Add to Review Set, doc corrections) is unaffected — ships as already kicked off. **Doctrine adopted into `AGENTS.md` 2026-07-31**, folded into `v0.68.0` alongside the Topic Note Rename — the doctrine text itself (the three bullets above) needed no further decision, matching the Gate below; the `EXAM_MODES.md` gap stays open, still tied to actual Phase 1 execution which remains blocked on Phase 0 | **Doctrine text: shipped, `AGENTS.md`'s new "Companion Guidance Doctrine" section, 2026-07-31.** Any further phase (Phase 1 audit/merge onward) still needs Phase 0 (Primary-vs-Focus) resolved first, and its own `/kickoff` before implementation | 2026-07-31 |

Owner's reasoning: near-zero community publishing likely exists *because* creators get nothing back for it — the chicken-and-egg counter `09`'s own analysis already named as a live, un-resolved branch ("this could be a small lever on the publishing rate, not only a reward waiting for scale"), not a new argument being waved through. Weighed against months of flat retention with no lever having moved it yet, owner chose to try this rather than keep waiting on more data. **On record, not a blocker:** `09`'s own "Answers to the memo's 12 questions" §11 frames this as a creator/ecosystem feature with at best a second-order retention effect, explicitly *not* a lever on the general ~2.4% W1→W2 problem the Diagnostic Read (due ~2026-08-06) is the actual read on — worth having visible given retention is the stated motivation here, but the owner's call stands regardless. **Checkpoint committed 2026-07-28 (strategic reprioritization discussion):** treat this as a bet with a re-check, not an indefinite ship-and-forget — re-run the gate query (`14-knowledge-impact-gate-check.sql` or its successor) 30-60 days after this ships, and treat a continued-zero or still-collapsed community-publish rate as settling the "can we create creators" hypothesis for good, not a reason to keep waiting. Worth being explicit that the only trend data available *right now* (monthly community-publish rate 1→3→0 across May/June/July, zero in the highest-signup month on record) argues against the "dormant creators waiting for a reason to publish" story rather than being neutral — this ratification is a deliberate bet against the most recent data point, not a bet made in an information vacuum. See the "Creator Loop" entry in the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc. Prior gate-query history, superseded by this ratification: **Parked, data-gated. Gate query run 2026-07-28 — failed, more concentrated than the original inference, not less.** `docs/claude-prompt/company-redefinition-out/14-knowledge-impact-gate-check.sql` (wider Java-level official-author definition — email-or-admin-role, not the narrower SQL predicate, see that file's own inconsistency note): 3 distinct non-official public-note creators (threshold was order-of-magnitude 20-30+); 697 official public notes vs. 4 community (99.4% official); views/copies split 12,835/2,994 official vs. 231/2 community (~1.8%/~0.07% community share); monthly community-publish rate 1 (2026-05), 3 (2026-06), zero (2026-07). Separately confirmed while writing that query, still unfixed: `PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl`'s SQL-level official-author predicate checks admin-role only, missing the email-based check `PublicProfileService.isOfficialAuthor` (Java) uses — the public library's own OFFICIAL/COMMUNITY filter likely mis-buckets the owner's personally-authored public notes as community today | **[DECISION — made 2026-07-28] + [CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11].** Checked against the 2026-07-28 bootstrap test and passes clause 1 (Knowledge Impact is the only lever that rewards publishing — waiting for publish-rate evidence before building the thing meant to move publish rate is circular); clause 2 (one release slot, reversible — a dashboard feature, no schema lock-in beyond the new event); clause 3 requires the checkpoint itself to be a conditional rate, not the same 3-creator raw count re-run — see below. Kill criterion: a continued-zero or still-collapsed community-publish rate on 2026-09-11 settles "can we create creators" for good, no further deliberation. `09`'s "Answers to the memo's 12 questions" and "Risks to avoid, if/when this is un-parked" sections are the design brief once scoped: passive/pull dashboard (not notifications), retrospective + aggregate framing ("helped," never "ranked"), weight downstream signal (led to N study sessions) over raw view/copy counts, nothing comparative/ranked/real-time/identifying across creators, private-to-the-creator-themselves, no streaks/badges/leaderboards, optional low-frequency opt-in digest reusing the existing Email Preferences category system. On a 3-creator base specifically, absolute per-creator counts ("helped 4 learners") read very differently depending on framing — treat the low-N reality as a design input, not a reason to wait. **Scoping requirement, not optional:** ship a conditional-rate `AnalyticsEventType` (did a creator who viewed their impact dashboard publish again within N days) in the same release — re-running the raw 3-creator-count query in September is the same unmeasurable-checkpoint failure v0.48.0's digest lift already hit; see the gate-types note above | 2026-07-28, kicked off as `v0.62.0` same day; shipped and signed off 2026-07-29 — see `RELEASES.md` v0.62.0 and `docs/releases/v0.62.0.md`. The 2026-09-11 checkpoint remains open, not yet run. |

| **Onboarding redesign — the first run as product comprehension, not account setup** | Owner-raised 2026-08-11, after `v0.72.1`'s activation read. Consultation brief: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-redesign-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md`. Design direction and screen-by-screen recommendations: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-redesign-ux-review.md`. Funnel numbers: `docs/claude-plans/v0.72.1-activation-read.sql` | **Design direction only — nothing scoped, no version opened.** The evidence: **132 learners, 35.2% of all signups**, verify their email and never finish onboarding — the largest single drop anywhere in the funnel. The owner's framing is that onboarding reads as a settings wizard and should instead be the first thing that communicates what NoteLib is. **Three findings from the design pass change the shape of the work.** (1) **The post-generation flow is not what the brief assumed**: onboarding does not auto-start Quick Review (that concern was never real), but it also does not land the learner on their Study Pack — it ends on a three-CTA completion screen, whose first CTA already resolves to `/study-packs/{id}`. So the recommendation is *delete an interruption*, not *add a celebration*, and it removes a screen without removing a step. (2) **Universal onboarding as proposed would break the flow**: step 4 calls `/notes/generate` and `/notes/{id}/generate`, both of which call `requireEmailVerified`, so running onboarding before verification 403s at the exact moment it promises the first Study Pack. The counter-proposal is to move the verification ask to step 4 — from the door to the payoff — which needs no backend change. (3) **Steps 3 and 4 are copy and layout over shipped machinery** (the `v0.71.0` slice-5 Intent Router), so those asks are far cheaper than the brief implies | **`[DECISION]`** — and the decision is *on what grounds*, not whether the defect is real. **This work cannot be justified as a retention lever and must not be scoped as one.** `v0.72.1` measured that activation is already **52.2%**, which structurally caps activation-volume work below the retention lever regardless of the retention rate; the volume hypothesis was tested against a pre-committed rule and failed. The legitimate justification is **comprehension and first impression** — 132 people meet the product and leave before understanding it — which is a real reason and a different one. **Recorded plainly because this is the third justification offered for overlapping work:** it was proposed as a retention lever (read killed it), then as activation-volume recovery (arithmetic killed it), now as comprehension. The owner should weigh that pattern knowingly rather than have it pass silently. **Overlaps the Onboarding Intent Router row above — C8 and C9 are the same defects this brief reaches independently from a positioning angle; scope them once, not twice.** **One thing no read can currently answer:** *which* step the 132 abandon on — `profile_type` persists only at the final step and clean step events start 2026-07-28 (n≈5). The design pass is therefore whole-flow by necessity; if a narrower scope is wanted, instrument first — the events already exist and the denominator grows on its own  **SHIPPED as `v0.73.0`, signed off and deployed 2026-08-12.** Eight of ten planned items landed; **item 3 (removing the exam-date question) was never built** — found by the pre-signoff pressure test rather than by the per-item workflow, which had no step that re-read the scope list. **On review 2026-08-12 the item was decided against and closed**, because the post-session prompt that justified removal reaches only session-completers while onboarding reaches everyone; the claim was reworded to "one *required* question per screen" instead. **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]` — deploy-relative (deployed 2026-08-12), owner may override.** **Metric:** onboarding completion rate for signups created ON OR AFTER the deploy, against the **62.4%** (234/375) baseline — computed from `users.onboarding_completed_at`, never from `ONBOARDING_V2_COMPLETED`, per the `AGENTS.md` rule. **Cohort-scoping is load-bearing:** the redesign only reaches learners who onboard after deploy, so an all-time read would be diluted by the pre-deploy population and understate any effect. **Denominator is adequate, unlike the retention checkpoints** — signups run ~120/month, so ~120 is readable at 30 days; this is a rate over signups, not a cohort of 31. **Kill criterion:** if post-deploy completion is not measurably above 62.4%, the comprehension hypothesis this release was justified on is **unsupported** — reopen the framing rather than iterating on further onboarding polish. **If completion DROPS, the 5→8 screen split is the first suspect**, and the per-step funnel shipped as item 10 is what localises it. **Instrumentation verified emitting:** `ONBOARDING_V2_STEP_VIEWED` fires per screen with nine distinct `step_name`s, and the admin funnel reads completion from the `users` table. **One measurement wrinkle, recorded:** the step aggregation is unwindowed (a Known Limitation), so post-deploy scoping needs a windowed query or a delta against the figures recorded at signoff. | 2026-08-12 |
| **`v0.74.0` — does the perfect-score gate work as a progression, or as a wall?** | Pre-declared at the `v0.74.0` kickoff, 2026-08-12; dated at signoff 2026-08-13 | **Shipped and instrumented.** The Quiz tab is locked until a learner scores a verified-perfect Quick Review. The *integrity* half of the rationale is code-verified and owes nothing — the answer key demonstrably existed. **The *progression* half is the falsifiable one:** that gating the saved questions behind a perfect score improves the study journey rather than stranding people. **Instrumentation verified emitting, not merely declared:** `QUICK_REVIEW_MASTERED` (`QuickReviewSessionService`), `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_UNLOCKED` (same, once per learner+pack, now firing on unknown prior state too), `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_TAB_OPENED_AFTER_UNLOCK` (`private-note-detail-page-client.tsx`) | **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]`** — deploy+30 (deployed 2026-08-13). **Primary:** among learners starting Quick Review post-deploy, the fraction reaching a verified-perfect score — the unlock rate. **Kill criterion:** a low unlock rate means the gate is a wall rather than a progression → **relax it to "any completed Quick Review"**, which this release's own reasoning already establishes is sufficient for integrity; do not iterate on lock copy instead. **⚠️ Read the secondary metric as the real test.** The pre-deploy `a3` read showed **67.2% of sessions already scored perfect** and 92.0% reached `>= 4/5`, so the unlock rate will very likely come back high and the kill criterion is unlikely to fire — a high rate mostly confirms that perfect scores are common, not that the progression works. **Secondary: does an unlocked learner ever open the Quiz tab?** That tests whether the reward is *wanted* rather than merely reachable. **⚠️ WINDOW SPLIT BY THE `v0.78.0` DEPLOY — recorded 2026-08-14, BEFORE the read, and the timing is the point.** `v0.78.0` fills the mastered result screen's empty secondary slot with a next-Study-Plan-item suggestion, which competes for the click this secondary metric measures. **The metric is UNCHANGED and the kill criterion is UNCHANGED** — what changes is that September's reader must compare **within** sub-windows (pre- and post-`v0.78.0` deploy), not across the discontinuity. **The confound is real but marginal, and that was verified rather than assumed:** `post-session-next-step.tsx` carries **no Quiz-tab link and no unlock affordance** — unlock discovery happens on Note Detail (`private-note-detail-page-client.tsx`, which is where `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_TAB_OPENED_AFTER_UNLOCK` fires), and the mastered branch's **primary** action has routed away to `Take a Challenge` since `v0.74.0` shipped it in the same release that declared this checkpoint. A secondary action beside an already-diverting primary changes the degree of diversion, not its kind. **A suppression gate was considered and rejected:** showing the suggestion only on repeat mastered sessions would protect the first impression but not this metric, since *"ever opens"* is precisely what later sessions decide — partial protection at the cost of real coupling. **Denominator clause:** too small to read is itself the finding, not grounds to extend | 2026-08-14 |
| **`v0.74.0` — did removing the Quick Review → Adaptive Practice route cost Adaptive Practice adoption?** **⚠️ DEPLOY-SPLIT CAVEAT, RECORDED AT THE `v0.107.0` KICKOFF (2026-09-03), BEFORE THE READ.** That release's item 2 adds **Subject-Plan- and Review-Set-scoped Adaptive Practice** — a NEW way to start an `ADAPTIVE` session — **inside this checkpoint's measurement window.** So a RISE in Adaptive Practice adoption after the `v0.107.0` deploy is **not evidence that the removed Quick Review route did not matter**, and the two populations must be counted separately by entry point (`ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED`'s `entry` metadata, which `v0.76.1` added for exactly this purpose). **⚠️ AND THE KILL CRITERION ITSELF IS BEING RE-SPECIFIED BY `v0.107.0` ITEM 3: the remedy becomes *accelerate the dashboard recommendation*, NEVER *restore a demoted entry point*, because restoring it would undo the ratified recommendation-engine direction that `v0.107.0` item 2 implements.** | Owner decision 2026-08-13, taken on judgment rather than evidence: a 5-question static refresher is too weak a signal to spend quota-limited LLM remediation on. `EXAM_MODES.md` amended in the same change | **Shipped.** Quick Review result screens no longer offer Adaptive Practice in any form — not as the mastered-branch secondary, not in the fallback, not as the quota-exhausted upgrade prompt. The reasoning that it stays discoverable elsewhere is **verified in code**: the Dashboard Today Focus routes to it from both the due-concepts and weak-concepts branches, and the mode-selection screen still lists it. **Instrumentation pre-exists and emits:** `ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED` (`QuickReviewAdaptivePracticeService.java:201`) | **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]` — REMEDY RE-SPECIFIED 2026-08-14, BEFORE the read, and the timing is the point.** Changing a kill criterion *after* seeing the data would be rationalisation; changing it beforehand, with the reason recorded, is a decision. This is the latter — the owner ratified the *Adaptive Practice as the recommendation engine* direction on 2026-08-14, and the original remedy contradicted it, so it is corrected now rather than discovered in September. **The metric is UNCHANGED and still worth reading:** `ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED` per active learner, post-deploy vs the equivalent pre-deploy window (deploy+30, deployed 2026-08-13), read alongside the row above. **~~Old remedy: restore a demoted entry point.~~ NEW REMEDY — the result now changes SEQUENCING, not placement.** **If starts fall materially:** the Quick Review route *was* carrying real discovery. Under the ratified direction the answer is **not** to restore a link — it is that **the dashboard recommendation must carry that discovery load, and must be built and proven BEFORE any further entry point is removed.** A fall therefore makes the recommendation engine a **prerequisite** for the Challenge Quiz removal rather than an independent improvement, and raises the bar on how good it has to be. **If starts hold:** the route was not carrying discovery, the existing dashboard surfaces already suffice, and the Challenge Quiz removal is low-risk. **⚠️ Do not remove the Challenge Quiz entry point before this reads.** `v0.74.0` removed the Quick Review route; a second removal inside the same window confounds the two and destroys the read instead of answering it. **Secondary question — MEASUREMENT PREREQUISITE SHIPPED IN `v0.76.1`.** The ratified direction rests on learners discovering remediation *from the dashboard*. `ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED` now records an allowlisted entry value across **every** route link that can start a session — Dashboard Today's Focus, Dashboard Focus Areas, **Dashboard Continue**, Challenge Quiz result, Interview Practice gap, **Note Detail's mode launch**, **Note Detail's due-concepts prompt**, or explicit `direct` — so Dashboard-originated starts can be separated from Note Detail and genuinely direct ones. **The three Dashboard values matter together:** a pre-signoff audit found `dashboard-continue` untagged, which would have recorded expired-session restarts as `direct` and **understated the very figure this read exists to measure.** Unknown caller-controlled values normalize to `direct` rather than being persisted verbatim. **This additive field does not disturb the unchanged primary before/after count**, which is a total. **If dashboard-originated starts turn out to be near zero, the dashboard-first premise is in trouble before anything is built on it**, so this is the read that would actually falsify the new direction rather than confirm it. **Denominator clause unchanged:** Adaptive Practice is quota-limited (Free 3/mo), so volumes are low by construction — too small to read is the finding, not a reason to extend. **⚠️ DELIVERY ANNOTATION added at the `v0.80.0` signoff, 2026-08-15, before the read.** The **primary** metric (`ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED`) is **backend-fired and unaffected**. The **secondary** (`STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_TAB_OPENED_AFTER_UNLOCK`) is frontend-fired, and this window opened 2026-08-13 while `v0.80.0` fixed frontend analytics delivery on ~2026-08-15 — so roughly **3 of 30 days ran on a pipeline that silently dropped events after token expiry**, biased toward *clicks* rather than impressions. Read the secondary as a **floor**, not a rate. **Conditional on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-14]` (`v0.80.0`): if that read shows delivery volume was materially unchanged, this annotation is void — strike it rather than reasoning around it** **⚠️ QUERY RE-SPECIFIED AT THE `v0.107.0` KICKOFF (2026-09-03), BEFORE THE READ — the REMEDY is untouched, only the metric's DEFINITION is narrowed.** `v0.107.0` item 2 adds **plan- and Review-Set-scoped Adaptive Practice**, a NEW way to start an `ADAPTIVE` session, **inside this checkpoint's measurement window.** This metric is a **BARE COUNT** of `ADAPTIVE_PRACTICE_STARTED` per active learner and **does NOT read entry metadata**, so those starts would **silently inflate it** and the checkpoint would report *starts held* while the NEW surface did the work — **answering a question about the removed Quick Review route with evidence produced by a feature that did not exist when the question was asked.** **⚠️ SO COUNT ONLY THE PRE-EXISTING NOTE-SCOPED `entry` VALUES** (`dashboard-today-focus`, `dashboard-focus-areas`, `challenge-quiz-result`, `interview-practice-gap`, `dashboard-continue`, `note-detail`, `note-detail-due-concepts`, `direct`) **and EXCLUDE the plan-scoped entries item 2 adds.** This is possible only because `v0.76.1` put `entry` on this event for exactly this purpose. **⚠️ The kill criterion and both branches of the remedy are UNCHANGED.** | 2026-08-13 |
| **Post-mastery next step — suggest a related note or the learner's next Study Plan item** | Owner-raised 2026-08-13 while reviewing `v0.74.0` PR #1059 ("when mastered, can we make it suggest a similar note or what was in Study Plan?"). No planning doc yet | **LEG (a) SHIPPED in `v0.78.0` (signed off 2026-08-15), and the release grew past it.** Leg (a) shipped as specified; sizing it against production then showed it can fire for at most **31% of packed-note learners** (69% have nothing in a plan), so the release also added a named program-matched plan recommendation on the Dashboard and in the same post-mastery slot. **(b) remains deferred** behind `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]`. Conversion of the new recommendation is measured by `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-14]`. **Originally: LEG (a) SCOPED INTO `v0.78.0`, 2026-08-14 — the a/b decision this row demanded is now taken: (a) only.** (b) stays deferred behind `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]` and folds into the Discovery System, exactly as this row instructed. **"Next" was defined at the same time (owner decision): lowest `NoteCollectionItemEntity.position` the learner has not practiced, reusing `toProgressResponse`'s existing `lastSessionCompletedAt != null`** — so the suggestion cannot contradict the plan's own progress counter. All three of this row's code claims were verified at kickoff and hold. **Scoped and sized, not started. `v0.74.0` left the slot open on purpose:** once a learner masters a pack, the result screen advances to `Take a Challenge` and now carries **no secondary action** at all — Adaptive Practice was removed from that screen in the same release. That empty slot is exactly where this would land, so the idea has a home rather than needing one invented. **Two different features hide inside the one question, and they are not the same size.** **(a) "Next item in this Study Plan" — moderate, and the cheaper half.** `TodayFocusType.STUDY_SUGGESTION` already exists but is only `DashboardService`'s generic empty-state fallback ("Create your first Study Pack"); **there is no next-note query anywhere.** Needs a definition of what "next" means (plan position? readiness? simply unstudied?), a repository query, a new branch in `PostSessionNextStepService`, and an empty state for the many notes that belong to no plan. **(b) "A similar note" — bigger, and it collides with committed work.** Similarity needs subject/tag/program overlap or embeddings, which is the **Discovery System** initiative already in this table and deliberately deferred until the Explore checkpoint reports. Building a second recommender beside it would fragment both. | **`[DECISION]` + `[EFFORT]`.** Decide **which** feature is wanted before any scoping pass — they share a sentence and nothing else. (a) is unblocked and needs only a scoping pass. **(b) should not start before `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]` (Explore engagement) reports**, for the same attributability reason that defers the Discovery System itself, and should then be folded into that initiative rather than built standalone. | 2026-08-13 |
| **Math notation renders as raw LaTeX in quiz content** — owner-reported 2026-08-12 from the Practice Quiz surface | Diagnosis and full scope in this doc's `## v0.74.0 — Quiz Progression` section, "Folded in 2026-08-12". No separate planning doc | **Folded into `v0.74.0` by owner decision 2026-08-12.** Root cause is a contract that was never written down: the KaTeX renderer only activates inside `$…$` / `\(…\)` / `$$…$$` / `\[…\]`, and **none of the 19 prompt files ever tells the model to emit delimiters**, so it freelances inconsistently *within a single quiz* — Unicode subscripts, bare carets, bare `\frac`. **Production sizing 2026-08-12: of 5,472 packs, ~23 are affected (~0.4%).** Three parts: **A** prompts (root cause, new content), **B** renderer normalisation (fixes all stored content at display time, zero data risk, and alone resolves every reported case), **C** admin backfill over the quiz JSON only — `question`, `choices`, `answer`, `explanation`, `workingSolution`, owner-confirmed | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12 — A and B ship, Part C is DROPPED by owner decision.** The sizing settled it: Part C was a new endpoint, a Java normaliser, session guards and idempotency **to repair ~23 packs that Part B already fixes on screen**, and it recreated the two-implementations-of-one-rule hazard PR 1's audit caught (a PL/pgSQL scorer diverging from `QuizItem` would have locked out every learner). **The owner regenerates the affected notes by hand** via `docs/claude-plans/v0.74.0-latex-affected-notes.sql`, which lists them per note with a `verdict` column and a companion query showing the offending text. That also keeps regeneration an explicit per-note act, as `CLAUDE.md`'s never-auto-regenerate rule prefers. **Do not re-propose Part C without a materially larger affected population**; if revived, the non-negotiables are recorded in the release section above | 2026-08-12 |
| **`v0.74.0` proposal — Quiz progression, and two deliberately deferred candidates** | Owner-raised 2026-08-12 after `v0.73.0` shipped; refined the same day. Consultation brief: `docs/claude-plans/next-release-candidates-consultation-prompt.md` | **Planning only — nothing scoped, no version open. Candidate 1 (Quiz progression) is live; candidates 2 (public Explore) and 3 (verification flow) are DEFERRED by owner decision until their checkpoints report.** **The rationale for candidate 1 changed on a code finding and is now much stronger than the original "progression feels nicer" framing:** `practice-quiz-card.tsx:25` renders the saved quiz with `revealAnswer`, so **the Quiz tab is an answer key** — and Quick Review administers those same questions (`note.quiz`). A learner can read every answer and then be "assessed" on them, which makes the score meaningless *and* corrupts `ConceptHealth`, locked since v0.37.0 to move only from genuine assessment. **It also resolves what would otherwise look arbitrary** — locking the easier artifact while leaving Challenge Quiz open is correct precisely because Challenge generates its own questions, so the answer key cannot spoil it. Proposal: lock (not hide) the Quiz tab until mastery, keep Challenge always available, celebrate the unlock, replace "Finish Review" with a return-to-note action, exempt curators, and instrument six events | **KICKED OFF as `v0.74.0` 2026-08-12 — planning row closed, scope now lives in `RELEASES.md` and this doc's own `## v0.74.0` section.** **Two owner rulings at kickoff changed the shape from what this row described:** (1) **nothing is parked — all seven items ship, including item 4**, which this row had recorded as time-blocked until 2026-09-30; the cost and its mitigation are in the Challenge Quiz adoption row above. (2) **Item 5's open session-lifecycle question is settled: "Review the Notes" COMPLETES the session, then navigates.** The premise that Quick Review no longer has a session was checked and does not hold — `completeSession` writes `ConceptHealth` and `COMPLETED_QUICK_REVIEW`, and this release's own 5/5 gate is a completed-session fact. **Candidates 2 (public Explore) and 3 (verification flow) stay DEFERRED** until their checkpoints report. Historical record follows. **`[DECISION]`** — **fully specified as of 2026-08-12; ready to kick off.** **Mastery = a PERFECT 5/5, and a perfect score reached through *Redo Mistakes* counts** (both owner rulings, 2026-08-12) — 4/5 does not qualify, but the gate is reachable by every learner who persists, which is what keeps it from being a dead end. **The product-UX second opinion is IN, not pending** — the owner confirmed the refined proposal *was* that read; do not re-send the brief for consultation despite its "consultation-prompt" filename. **Grade the settled items honestly:** the mastery rulings are explicit owner decisions; locking the tab, keeping Challenge open, expressing the progression as a locked tab, and shipping the "Review the Notes" item are settled *by adoption* — they were carried in the returned proposal, not ruled on individually. **Two prior open items closed:** (a) the lock copy was contradicting the gate (*"Complete Quick Review to unlock"* while the gate was perfect) and has been rewritten to name the real condition and point a 4/5 learner at Redo Mistakes; (b) **the "Quick Review length varies by pack" concern is retracted for anything generated under current code, with ONE CHECK STILL OUTSTANDING.** Newly generated quizzes are always exactly 5 — `schema.json` pins `minItems/maxItems: 5`, `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_QUESTION_COUNT = 5` (`OpenAiLlmStudyPackService.java:64`), and generation is rejected at a different count (`:432`). **But Quick Review takes `totalQuestions` straight from the stored row** (`QuickReviewSessionService.java:98`, no slicing), and that validation only landed **2026-03-18** (`c78ee9f1`) — older packs, and remixes/copies descended from them (`ShareService.java:90`, `NoteService.java:401`), were never subject to it. **Run `docs/claude-plans/v0.74.0-quiz-length-check.sql` before writing learner-facing copy:** a single row of 5 makes "Score 5/5" exact; anything else forces length-agnostic wording (*"answer every question correctly"*). **A distinction worth keeping at implementation time:** the *integrity* rationale (do not hand someone the answer key to a test they have not sat) is satisfied by any completed Quick Review, since the first attempt at each question is the genuine assessment `ConceptHealth` records — the perfect-score requirement is the *progression* layer on top, not the integrity fix. **Still open, a scoping call not a design one:** whether "Review the Notes" completes, pauses, or discards the session, since it moves when `QUICK_REVIEW_COMPLETED` fires and that event feeds the admin funnel's value-loop metric — decide before writing that item's Codex prompt. **One sub-item is time-blocked:** promoting Challenge to the primary CTA on mastery moves the `>= 4/5` threshold that the `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-30]` measures, which would destroy that read — the other six sub-items are unblocked | 2026-08-12 |

## Single Program Axis — remove "Primary Course / Program" (ratified 2026-08-05, scoped into `v0.71.0` as **slice 4**)

**An architectural simplification, not a feature.** Ratified after Release B shipped and the two-field model met real use. Binding text lives in `ADR-001` → "Course / Program(s) is the only program concept"; this section is the scoping view.

**Why it was added to `v0.71.0` after kickoff — a deliberate exception, not drift.** Signoff *is* deploy, and two things that make this change free expire at exactly that moment: **no one has learned the two-field model** (V107 has never reached production, so teachers would otherwise learn it and then re-learn it), and **no multi-program note exists anywhere** (so the Domain Context requirement lands with zero violations and no backfill). Sequencing it after signoff would also mean the expensive whole-release pressure test runs against an intermediate architecture and then again against the real one. It simplifies what this release built rather than extending it.

**All four note-creating surfaces become many-valued — owner ruling 2026-08-05.** Note Editor, Note Detail inline panel, Create-from-topic, and Bulk Generate. The narrower "editor surfaces only" option was offered and declined. **Known consequence, accepted:** because the Domain Context requirement keys on program count, a Bulk Generate batch spanning several programs now also requires a Domain Context — bulk authoring changes from "pick a program, generate" to "pick programs *and* a Domain Context."

**The reframing that settled it.** Every axis owns exactly one responsibility — Subject owns *what*, Domain Context owns *how it is authored*, Note Learner Level owns *how deeply*, Course / Program(s) owns *where it is discovered*. A "primary" program owns **none** of them; it is simply whichever program came first, an artifact of a schema that used to be single-valued. So the question was never "what should replace primary?" but **"should a concept with no remaining architectural responsibility exist?"** — and it should not.

**What ships when this is scoped:**

- **One picker, `Course / Program(s)`**, many-valued, catalog-backed, required. No separate Applicable Programs section, no primary, no synchronisation between two fields. Helper text carries the capability: *"Choose one or more programs this note applies to. Adding multiple programs lets one note serve several curricula instead of creating duplicates."*
- **Domain Context is generation readiness, not note validity, when a note has more than one program.** The multi-program/null-context state may be saved; every note-to-Study-Pack generation entry rejects it server-side before status, quota, rate-limit, or LLM side effects. A single-program note keeps the single-value fallback. **Sending the program list to the LLM is explicitly rejected** — the prompt's domain constraint is unsatisfiable given several disciplines, which is the founding reason Domain Context exists. Revisiting needs an R4-style read first.
- **Note Detail header stops implying a primary.** One line — *"Applicable to N programs"* — opening a popover (desktop) / bottom sheet (mobile) with the full list. **Same viewer on public note detail**, where it earns most: a learner arriving from a shared link never filtered by anything, so "does this fit my program?" is live for them. A single-program note just names it, with no control.
- **Library cards keep the count**, not names. The viewer on detail is what makes that defensible rather than lossy — names stay one tap away. Recommendation, not yet accepted: see the open question below.
- **`notes.course_program` is NOT dropped, and is NOT legacy.** Corrected 2026-08-05 after the authoring-UX pass: it is **the personal-notes program field** and is still written by learner authoring. `note_course_program` is the curated-content axis. An earlier framing in this section and in ADR-001 called it a frozen, write-never compatibility column with an exit condition — that assumed every author would be catalog-restricted, which is not the product. Full reasoning in ADR-001 → "Two authoring modes".
- **Two authoring modes, one gating line.** Curators (Teacher/Admin) get **catalog-only** programs and **one or many**; learners get **free text** and **exactly one**. Cardinality and vocabulary follow the same condition deliberately — splitting them differently would make the model harder to explain than either split alone. Multi-program authoring is a **curation act**, not a permission tier, and must be documented that way.

**Do this while it is free.** `V107` yields exactly one join row per note and has not reached production, so **no multi-program note exists anywhere**. The Domain Context requirement therefore lands with zero pre-existing violations and no backfill. That window closes the moment curators start authoring multi-program notes.

**Migration risks already identified** — none is a blocker, all need handling: live public shareable slug URLs for catalog-excluded values; public search matching `course_program` text; `users.course_program` as the last link in the domain fallback chain; and the irreversibility of dropping any column, which is why none is dropped here.

**Open question, not yet decided:** whether library cards should show program names (owner's instinct: `Civil Engineering • Mechanical Engineering • +2`) or the count (engineering recommendation: names read as a second identity beside the Subject badge, are largely redundant once a learner has filtered by program, and force an arbitrary truncation choice). A GPT consultation prompt covering this and the architecture is at `22-remove-primary-program-gpt-consultation-prompt.md`.

## Current Product Shape

Navigation:

- Main:
  - Dashboard
  - Library
  - Public Library
- Account:
  - Profile
  - Settings
  - Admin (admins only)

Core routes:

- `/dashboard`
- `/library`
- `/public/library`
- `/notes/{id}`
- `/notes/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}` for session review
- `/public/library/{subject}`
- `/public/library/{subject}/{slug}`
- `/public/profile/{userId}`

Current session-review UX:

- desktop and mobile both open the same dedicated session-review page from `Recent Sessions`
- Note Detail stays the entry point for history, while the dedicated review page owns focused answer review

## Future Directions

### Background Quiz Pre-Generation (de-scoped from v0.17.0)

Challenge Quiz was the original pooling candidate but was de-scoped: its progressive generation model (5 → 10 → 15 → 20 questions on demand) already limits the initial wait to a single 5-question LLM call, so pre-generation adds LLM cost without a meaningful UX benefit. Additionally, pre-generating the initial batch and then generating extensions on demand would introduce concurrency waste (pool generation and on-demand generation could race for the same note).

Revisit for a different mode if a cold-start bottleneck is confirmed by usage data — e.g., a mode with a fixed long question set where the full generation is the bottleneck (Long Exam already uses the `ExamQuestionPoolService` pool mechanism for this). Do not pre-generate Challenge Quiz.

**This ruling still holds and is not reversed by `v0.60.0`'s Challenge Quiz Official template seeding.** This ruling is about pre-warming one user's own progressive batch (5→10→15→20 on demand) ahead of their own on-demand "give me more" clicks — that would race against on-demand generation for no UX benefit, since the initial wait is already a single 5-question call. `v0.60.0` seeds a *shared* Official template exactly once per Official study pack (not per user, not per session) to eliminate N adopters each redundantly regenerating identical content from an LLM; every adopter still claims through the ordinary on-demand path (copy-on-claim from the template, or live generation if the template is exhausted), so there is no per-user pre-warm and no race with progressive generation. See `RELEASES.md` v0.60.0 and this doc's Phase 3 / `v0.60.0` sections above for the full design.

### Interview Practice evolution

Initial evaluation is promoted to the active v0.15.0 section. The following remain unsequenced follow-up directions and should not be committed until Interview Practice v1 has usage data from at least one billing cycle.

- **Multi-note Interview Practice (smart context aggregation)** — generate from the base note plus related notes that share `courseProgram` and at least one tag; cap at 2–3 sibling notes to manage prompt size and per-session cost; the dashboard entry already selects the most relevant note, so multi-note adds breadth across a topic, not a replacement for that smart selection; do not implement until v1 usage data confirms users want wider scenario coverage than a single note provides
- **Structured interview templates by role/job family** — opinionated section breakdowns (e.g. Backend Engineer = PL fundamentals + DB + Behavioral); requires either a curated role taxonomy or a user-defined template builder; do not build until v1 usage data shows real demand and the section-aware generation prompt's limitations are observed
- **Open-ended / conversational evaluation** — replace MC structure with free-text answers and AI rubric scoring; architecturally heavy (new session schema, new evaluation pipeline, new result model); only consider if MC + critique format hits its ceiling and Pro users explicitly ask for it
- **Profile / role enrichment** — capture target role explicitly on the user profile (instead of inferring from notes) to drive better generation context; bigger architectural decision; do not bundle with any of the above — design separately
- **Interview Practice tier promotion to Plus** — only if v0.14.0 usage data justifies the LLM cost; current model split (gpt-4.1 generation + gpt-4.1-mini critique) is what makes Pro-only economically viable, and lowering the tier requires re-running that math

### Lesson Plan for Teachers (future, unsequenced)

A lightweight collection entity grouping an ordered set of notes into a lesson plan for teacher-profile users. No AI synthesis at the plan level — a lesson plan is a playlist, not a synthesized document.

- New `LessonPlan` entity: title, description, ordered list of note references with optional week/topic labels per item
- No new AI generation at plan creation; Study Pack and Quiz generation still happen per-note using existing quotas — no new quota category
- Teacher dashboard gains a "Lesson Plans" section alongside the library; notes remain individually owned and independently editable
- DOCX export from a lesson plan produces a multi-section packet: one quiz section per note in lesson-plan order
- "Generate Quiz for Lesson Plan" toolbar action generates quizzes for each note in sequence, consuming the teacher's existing quiz quota per note
- Requires backend: new `LessonPlan` entity + ordering join table; frontend: lesson plan creation UI + DOCX multi-section export
- Do not implement Option B (multi-note AI synthesis across all notes in the plan) in v1 — risk of lower-quality synthesis and significantly higher LLM cost per plan; Option A (collection model) delivers the organization and sequencing value teachers need without new AI spend

### Study Pack Section Improvements (future, unsequenced)

The current Study Pack format (Overview / Key Idea / Core Details / Why It Matters / Quick Recall) is consistent but template-locked — every note produces the same five sections regardless of subject or content type. Planned improvements in order of effort:

- **Common Misconceptions section** — names what students typically get wrong about the topic; high quiz-prep value; prompt-only, no schema change; hotfix-deployable
- **Richer Quick Recall** — expand term-definition pairs to include a memory hook (analogy, mnemonic, or visual cue); prompt-only; hotfix-deployable
- **Comparison tables** — when the note contrasts two or more concepts, generate a structured markdown table instead of parallel bullet lists; prompt-only; hotfix-deployable
- **Concept relationships** — prerequisite chains ("Understand X before this") and contrast pointers ("Different from Y because..."); prompt-only; hotfix-deployable
- **Subject-adaptive section templates** — STEM notes get an Equations + Variables block and a Worked Example; humanities notes get a Timeline or Key Arguments section; may require a `sectionType` field addition to the key concept schema if the current JSONB storage cannot accommodate variable section shapes; requires a design pass before implementation

The first four improvements are prompt-only and can ship as hotfixes without schema changes. Subject-adaptive templates require a design review on the key concept schema before implementation and should not be bundled with the prompt-only items.

### Connected Account Management / Auth Provider Management (Future)

Future improvements for users with multiple sign-in methods. The Google OAuth foundation (account linking, `email_verified` guard, Profile sign-in method status) is in place; these are follow-on improvements that require their own design pass before implementation.

Potential scope:

- unlink Google account safely — must prevent lockout when Google is the only sign-in method
- add/change password for Google-only users — allow switching to or adding email/password without forcing a full re-signup
- multiple provider support — Apple, Microsoft, etc.; do not add until Google is stable and provider abstraction is reviewed
- connected account security UX — notify users by email when a new provider is linked to their account
- account recovery flows for social-login users — what happens when Google revokes access or the associated email changes
- provider conflict resolution UI — surface a clear choice when a Google email matches an existing email/password account
- recent login/session visibility — show sign-in history and active sessions in Settings for security-aware users
- email change flow with connected providers — changing the NoteLib email when a Google account is linked needs careful sequencing

Implementation notes for future reference:

- the provider abstraction should live in a shared auth provider layer, not scattered across login and signup flows
- lockout prevention: never allow unlinking the only auth method unless an alternative is confirmed first
- Google-only users have no password; the "add password" flow must go through a secure email-based credential creation path
- do not add more providers until the connected-account management UX is designed; adding providers without management UX creates a support burden

### Public Library persona filtering (roadmap)

Planned for a future release after the mode system matures:

- persona-based note recommendations in Public Library discovery (same-profile notes ranked higher)
- cross-profile discovery still allowed so learners can find materials outside their profile type
- filtering UI: optional "Relevant to me" toggle that uses the current user's profile mode for ranking
- implementation must remain additive — no ranking change without the toggle enabled
- do not build until there are enough public notes per profile type to make filtering meaningful

## Known UX Fixes (cross-cutting, no version gate)

These are correctness fixes that ship as soon as they are ready and are not held to a version milestone.

- ~~**AI suggestion modal survives navigation**~~ ✅ — fixed in v0.15.1 branch: `awaitingGeneratedMetadataSuggestionRef` was in-memory-only and reset on component remount; navigating away mid-generation and returning silently skipped the AI title/subject/tags modal; replaced with a `sessionStorage` key (`notelib-awaiting-suggestion:{noteId}`) that is set when generation starts and cleared after the modal fires; `loadDetail` re-arms the ref from storage when returning to a still-generating note so the polling effect can still trigger the modal on completion.

## Product Learning Loop

Three loops, not one — updated 2026-07-28 to replace the single-line notes-first loop below, which predates the journey-centric direction (see "Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame" near the top of this doc for the reasoning). Roadmap decisions should reinforce these loops rather than one-time output generation; naming something a loop doesn't make it spin, so each is tagged with its actual current status, not aspirational status.

- **Learning Loop — running, but "proven" was unfounded, corrected 2026-07-28.** Practice → Improve → Progress/Readiness → Confidence → Practice again. This is the `ConceptHealth`/readiness spine that's been the core mechanic since v0.33.0–v0.37.0, now backed by Reusable Practice Assets (v0.58.0) so repeat practice has something stable to reuse instead of regenerating every session. **The mechanic is real and has been running the longest of the three loops, but no cited evidence establishes it's actually driving retention or improvement outcomes** — an Opus pressure-test caught this doc previously labeling it "proven" with nothing behind that word, the exact failure mode (an unfalsified success claim sitting in the roadmap) this section's own honesty about the other two loops was supposed to guard against. Treat as "running, unverified" until something measures it directly.
- **Acquisition Loop — real, but asserted not measured.** Public Library / Facebook / Exam Hub → Official Review Set → Adopt → Practice. Supported by the 2026-07-24 signup surge (LET/Facebook-driven) and adoption concentration on two specific Review Sets, but the funnel mechanism itself (how much is in-app browse-to-adopt vs. direct-link-to-adopt) hasn't been measured end-to-end — treat as a working hypothesis, not a confirmed mechanic, until the Diagnostic Read's segmentation (see the Diagnostic Read section above) clarifies it.
- **Creator Loop — not currently spinning; a bet, not a running loop.** Publish → Knowledge Impact → Motivation to contribute → Better public library → More adoption. Currently inert at the first stage: community publish rate went to zero in 2026-07, the highest-signup month on record. Knowledge Impact (ratified 2026-07-28, see its Backlog Index row and checkpoint) is a deliberate bet that the feature itself can restart this loop — don't treat it as an already-running mechanic in any roadmap reasoning until its own 30-60-day checkpoint shows real movement.

Original notes-first loop, preserved for history: Capture -> Generate -> Review -> Improve -> Copy -> Repeat. Still true at the single-note level; the three loops above are the layer above it.
